:
Logarithm excess loss of the Polyak-Ruppert iterate for a single client (
N=1
).

Interpretation - real datasets
(
Figures

7(c)
and
7(f)
). We observe that quantization performs competitively with PP and outperforms all other compressors.
The asymptotic behavior is consistent with
Figure

6
(top-row) for
\omega=1
, where the order
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}\ll\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}
is observed.
This experience is going beyond
Proposition

2
which only applies to
1
-quantization.

3.5
Conclusion

In this section, we investigated how the compression scheme choice impacts the convergence rate, first by showing that quantization-based and projection-based methods respectively satisfy
Theorem

1
and
Theorem

2
, resulting in different non-asymptotic behaviors. In the asymptotic regime, in both cases, the averaged excess loss scales as
\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{-1}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\right)/K
. We then analyzed the impact of the most-used schemes on this limit rate. Overall, it appears that all compression schemes typically generate an
unstructured-noise
, which covariance does not scale with
H
, contrarily to the classical un-compressed
1
. The one exception is PP, which corresponds (on a single worker) to performing fewer iterations. For other compression schemes, we show the impact of the covariance
H
: depending on the correlation between features (
H
diagonal or not) and on the pre-processing (e.g., standardization for which
H
has diagonal constant), the ordering between compression scheme varies.
In many cases, this highlights the need for an additional regularisation when running
2
: all compression schemes (but PP) result in a significant noise that accumulates along the low curvature directions. Our results can be extended to the ridge (a.k.a., Tikhonov) regularized case
(
Dieuleveut et al. 2017
, see)
, which creates an additional bias but changes the rate
\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{-1}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\right)/K
into
\mathrm{Tr}\left((H+\lambda\mathrm{I})^{-1}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\right)/K
. The theoretical optimal choice for
\lambda
depending on
H
and the compression scheme could be obtained from our analysis but is left as future work.

We now turn to the distributed/federated case, which motivates the study of compression schemes for practical applications.

4
Application to Federated Learning

In this section, we consider
3
under
1
, which corresponds to heterogeneous Federated Learning on a network composed of
N
clients. We hereafter consider two particular cases of
1
.
First, in
Section

4.1
, the
covariate-shift
case, i.e.,
1
with
w_{*}^{i}=w_{*}
for all
i
(thus the distribution of
y^{i}
conditional to
x^{i}
does not change between workers), but on the other hand, the features’ marginal distributions are different, in particular,
H_{i}\neq H_{j}
. Second, in
Section

4.2
, the
optimal-point-shift
case, i.e., for each client
i,j\in[N]
, their optimal points are different
w_{*}^{i}\neq w_{*}^{j}
, but
H_{i}=H_{j}
.
In the rest of the section, we denote
\overline{H}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}
,
\overline{R}^{2}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R^{2}_{i}
, and we have
F(w_{k})-F(w_{*})=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\eta_{k-1},\overline{H}\eta_{k-1}\right\rangle
.

4.1
Heterogeneous covariance

In this section, we first show that
Theorems

2
and
1
on (
LSA
) from
Section

2
can be applied to the Federated Learning case within the scenario of covariate-shift.
Corollary

4
is proved in
Section

F.1
.

Corollary 4

(
3
with covariate-shift)
.

Consider
3
under
1
with
w_{*}^{i}=w_{*}^{j}
(and potentially
H_{i}\neq H_{j}
).

1.

For a compressor
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sq}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
,

Theorem

1
holds, with
H_{F}=\overline{H}
,
R_{F}^{2}=\overline{R}^{2}
,
\mathcal{A}=(\omega+1)\overline{R}^{2}\sigma^{2}/N
,
\mathcal{M}_{2}=(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})/N
,
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\Omega\sigma\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})/N
.

2.

Moreover for any linear compressor
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
,
Theorem

2
holds, with the same constants and
\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}=\sigma^{2}\max_{i\in[N]}(\Sha_{H_{i}})/N
and
\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}=\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2}\Sha_{H_{i}})/N
, with
(\Sha_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N}
given in
Corollary

2
.

The Hessian of the objective function is now
\overline{H}
, and
Theorems

1
and
2
still hold. The proof consists in showing that with
Lemma

1
,
2
,
1
,
3
and
4
on the resulting random field
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are valid, with the constants given above.

In order to understand the impact of the compressor on the limit convergence rate, we establish a formula for
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
similar to
Equation

4
.
In the setting of covariate-shift, we have for any clients
i,j\in[N]
,
w_{*}^{i}=w_{*}^{j}
, thus

\displaystyle\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\overset{\text{def.}\penalty\ \ref{def:add_mult_noise}}{=}\xi_{k}(0)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{algo}\penalty\ \ref{ex:dist_comp_LMS}}{=}\nabla F(w_{*})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{*}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{eq.}\penalty\ \ref{eq:def_oracle}}{=}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}((\left\langle x_{k}^{i},w_{*}\right\rangle-y_{k}^{i})x_{k}^{i})\overset{\text{model}\penalty\ \ref{model:fed}}{\underset{\text{ with }w_{*}^{i}=w_{*}^{j}}{=}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i})\,.

Next for all operators under consideration we have
\mathcal{C}^{i}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i})\overset{\text{a.s.}}{=}\varepsilon_{k}^{i}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i})
, thus, with
p_{H_{i}}
denoting the distribution of
x_{k}^{i}
with covariance
H_{i}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\otimes 2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i})\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\right]\overset{\text{indep. of }(\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i})_{i=1}^{d}}{=}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]\overset{\mathrm{Def.\penalty\ \ref{def:cov_of_compression}}}{=}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i},p_{{H}_{i}})\overset{\text{notation}}{=:}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}\,.

(5)

The operator
\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}
generalizes the notion of
compressor’s covariance
(
Definition

5
) to the case of multiple clients, and
Equation

5
corresponds to
Equation

4
.

Remark 11

(All clients use the same
linear
compressor)
.

If for all
i\in[N]
,
\mathcal{C}^{i}\overset{(d)}{=}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}\}
, leveraging
Remark

10
, we have

\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C},{\overline{H}})\,.

The analysis of (
LSA
) on a single worker made in
Section

3
is still valid in this setting with now the Hessian of the problem being equal to the average of covariance
\overline{H}
.
Corollary

4
and
Equation

5
prove that the case of covariate-shift is identical to the centralized setting with a variance reduced by a factor
N
.

Remark 12

(Varying compressor, or compression level, or non-linear compression)
.

In most other cases, the computation of
\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{i},p_{{H}_{i}})
is possible using the results of
Section

3.3

Overall, in the covariate-shift case, most insights from the centralized case remain valid, especially, client sampling (i.e., PP) is the safest way to limit the impact of compression. Moreover, the trade-offs and ordering between compressors remain preserved, especially regimes in which quantization outperforms other competitors.

4.2
Heterogeneous optimal point

Hereafter, we focus on the case of heterogeneous optimal points and consider that all clients share the same covariance matrix, i.e. for any
i,j\in[N]
,
H_{i}=H
, but potentially
w_{*}^{i}\neq w_{*}^{j}
. This can be seen as a case of
concept-shift

(
Kairouz et al. 2019
)
, and we also refer to the situation as
optimal-point-shift
. This setting could eventually be combined with the covariate-shift case. Similarly,
Theorems

2
and
1
on (
LSA
) from
Section

2
can be applied.

Corollary 5

(
3
with concept-shift)
.

Consider
3
under
1
with
H_{i}=H_{j}
(and potentially
w_{*}^{i}\neq w_{*}^{j}
).

1.

For a compressor
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sq}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
,

Theorem

1
holds, with
H_{F}=H
,
R_{F}^{2}=R^{2}
,
\mathcal{A}=\frac{R^{2}(\omega+1)}{N}(\kappa\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\mathrm{Cov}\left[W_{*}\right]\right)+\sigma^{2})
with
W_{*}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{w_{*}^{i},i\in[N]\})
,
\mathcal{M}_{2}=(\omega+1)^{2}/N
, and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\Omega R^{2}\sigma/N
.

2.

Moreover for any linear compressor
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
,
Theorem

2
holds, with the same constants and
\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}=\sigma^{2}\Sha_{H}/N
and
\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}=R^{2}\Sha_{H}/N
, with
\Sha_{H}
given in
Corollary

2
.

Corollary

5
can be proved reusing computation made for
Corollary

4
and using below
Proposition

5
. We next aim at computing the additive noise covariance. We note
g_{k,*}^{i}=\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{*})
the local stochastic gradient evaluated at optimal point
w_{*}
. We have, in
1
, for any
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
F_{i}(w):=\mathbb{E}(\langle{x_{k}^{i}},{w-w_{*}^{i}}\rangle-x_{k}^{i}\varepsilon_{k}^{i})^{2}
, thus
\nabla F(w)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H(w-w_{*}^{i})
, and
w_{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}w_{*}^{i}/N
. The setting of
Definition

1
is verified with
H_{F}=H
, and for any
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, that the random field
\xi_{k}
can be computed as:

\displaystyle\xi_{k}(w-w_{*})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Def. }\ref{def:class_of_algo}\&\mathrm{Alg.}\ref{ex:dist_comp_LMS}}{=}H_{F}(w-w_{*})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w)),\text{ thus }\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\overset{\text{Def. }\ref{def:add_mult_noise}}{=}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i}),

with
g_{k,*}^{i}=(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})+x_{k}^{i}\varepsilon_{k}^{i}
. We thus have, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]\overset{\nabla F(w_{*})=0}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i})-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\forall i\neq j,\penalty\ \mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}\perp\mathcal{C}_{k}^{j}}{\underset{\mathbb{E}\mathcal{C}^{i}_{k}(g_{k,*}^{i})=\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})}{=}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{C}^{i}_{k}(g_{k,*}^{i})-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}^{i}_{k}(g_{k,*}^{i})^{{\otimes 2}}]-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})^{{\otimes 2}}\right)\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{{\Theta}_{i}})-\frac{\displaystyle 1}{\displaystyle N^{2}}H\sum_{i=1}^{N}(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})^{\otimes 2}H\preccurlyeq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{\Theta_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}\,,

where
p_{\Theta_{i}}
is the distribution of
g_{k,*}^{i}
(for any
k
). In the last inequality, we simply discarded the non-positive term
-H\sum_{i=1}^{N}(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})^{\otimes 2}H
. For linear compressors, by
Proposition

2
,
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
is a linear function of
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Theta_{i}
– the averaged second-order moment of the local gradients
(g_{k,*}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. In order to bound this quantity, following
Dieuleveut et al. 2017
, we make the following assumption.

Assumption 5
.

The kurtosis
for the projection of the covariates
x^{i}_{1}
(or equivalently
x^{i}_{k}
for any
k
) is bounded on any direction
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e., there exists
\kappa>0
, such that:

\forall i\in[N],\ \forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle z,x^{i}_{1}\right\rangle^{4}\right]\leq\kappa\langle z,Hz\rangle^{2}

For instance, it is verified for Gaussian vectors with
\kappa=3
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it implies that
\mathbb{E}[\left\langle z,x^{i}_{1}\right\rangle^{2}\ (x^{i}_{1})^{\otimes 2}]\preccurlyeq\kappa\langle z,Hz\rangle H
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 5

(Impact of client-heterogeneity.)
.

Let
W_{*}
be a random variable uniformly distributed over
\{w_{*}^{i},i\in[N]\}
, thus such that,
\mathrm{Cov}\left[W_{*}\right]=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})^{\otimes 2}
, then:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Theta_{i}\preccurlyeq(\kappa\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\mathrm{Cov}\left[W_{*}\right]\right)+\sigma^{2})\ H\,.

Proof

We have:

\displaystyle\Theta_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[((x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})+x_{k}^{i}\varepsilon_{k}^{i})^{\otimes 2}]\overset{(\varepsilon_{k}^{i})\perp(x_{k}^{i})}{=}\mathbb{E}[(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})^{\otimes 2}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})]+\sigma^{2}H

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Ass. }\ref{ass:kurtosis}}{\preccurlyeq}\kappa\left\langle w_{*}-w_{*}^{i},H(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})\right\rangle H+\sigma^{2}H=\kappa\mathrm{Tr}\left(H(w_{*}-w_{*}^{i})^{\otimes 2}\right)H+\sigma^{2}H\,.

In words, we have the following two main observations.

Remark 13

(
Structured noise before compression.
)
.

Before compression is possibly applied, the noise remains structured, i.e., with covariance proportional to
H
, in the case of concept-shift. As a consequence, the rate for un-compressed
Equation

LSA
will remain independent of the smallest eigenvalue of
H
. This remark extends to the case where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}
is applied.

Remark 14

(
Heterogeneous vs homogeneous case
)
.

Compared to the homogeneous case, in which
\Theta_{i}=\sigma^{2}H_{i}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{H_{i}})_{i=1}^{N})}
, the averaged second-order moment increases from
\sigma^{2}H
to
(\kappa\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\mathrm{Cov}\left[W_{*}\right]\right)+\sigma^{2})H
, showing the impact of the dispersion of the optimal points
(w_{*}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. This corresponds to the typical variance increase in the compressed heterogeneous SGD case
(
Mishchenko et al. 2019
;
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
)
.

Concept-shift thus hinders the limit convergence rate. To limit this effect, a solution is to introduce a control-variate term
(h^{i}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},i\in[N]}
, that is subtracted to the gradient before compression and asymptotically approximate
\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
for any
i\in[N]

(
Mishchenko et al. 2019
, see)
. We explore this direction in
Section

F.2
.

4.3
Numerical experiments

We support the theoretical results from
Sections

4.1
and
4.2
by performing experiments in the FL framework that extend the ones from
Section

3
.

On figures
Figure

8
, we present the results of the excess loss of the Polyak-Ruppert iterate
F(\overline{w}_{k})-F(w_{*})
versus the number of iterations in log/log scale. The experiments were run 5 times, each time with different datasets (dispersion is shown by shaded area).

Settings.
(a)
Synthetic dataset generation:
The dataset is generated using
1
with
N=10
,
K=10^{6}
on each client,
\sigma^{2}/{N}=1
. For any clients
i
in
[N]
, the covariance matrix is
H_{i}=Q_{i}D_{i}Q_{i}^{T}
, where
Q_{i}
is an orthogonal matrix.
For heterogeneous clients, the dataset generation is as follows.

Covariate shift:
The rotation matrix
Q_{i}
is sampled independently for each client and the diagonal matrix
D_{i}
is
\mathrm{Diag}\left((1/j^{\beta_{i}})_{j=1}^{d}\right)
where
\beta_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{3,4,5,6\})
.
Concept-shift:
The optimal models of the clients
i\in[N]
were drawn from a zero-centered normal distribution with a variance of
100\mathrm{I}_{d}
, that is,
w_{*}^{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,100\mathrm{I}_{d})
. We also take for all client
i
in
[N]
,
H_{i}=QDQ^{T}
, with
D=\mathrm{Diag}\left((1/j\right))_{j=1}^{d}
.
(b)
Real-dataset and covariate-shift:
To simulate non-i.i.d. clients, we split the dataset in heterogeneous
groups (with equal number of points) using a
K
-nearest neighbors clustering on the TSNE
representations
(
Maaten and Hinton 2008
, defined by)
. Thus,
the marginal feature distribution
significantly varies between clients, providing a covariate-shift, while keeping the same distribution for the output conditional to the features on all clients.
(c)
3
:

We take a constant step-size
\gamma=1/(2(\omega+1)R^{2})
with
R^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\right)
and
w_{0}=0
as initial point. We set the batch-size
b=1
for synthetic datasets and
b=16
for real datasets, the compressor variance is
\omega=10
.
(d)
3
vs
4
: We take a bigger constant step-size
\gamma=(2R^{2})^{-1}
in order to emphasize the difference between the case w./w.o. control variate, we set
w_{0}=0
as initial point and the compressor variance is
\omega=10
. We set the batch-size
b=32
for
Figure

8(c)
and
b=K
for
Figure

8(f)
.

Interpretation – homogeneous case and covariate-shift case
(
Figures

8(a)
,
8(d)
,
8(b)
and
8(e)
). These experiments extend those presented in
Section

3.4
in the case of a single client. The observations made in
the centralized case (
Figure

7
), especially on the impact of the compressor choice on the convergence and the ordering between limit convergence rates remain valid. This illustrates
Corollary

4
and
Remark

11
:
Theorems

2
and
1
hold in the case of homogeneous client or in the case of heterogeneous covariance and the only compressor that ensures that the noise is structured is client sampling (partial participation). On the real datasets, quantization is also competitive.

Interpretation – concept-shift case
(
Figures

8(c)
and
8(f)
). These experiments extend those presented on
Figure

7(e)
(slow eigenvalues’ decay with
\mu=10^{-2}
) to the scenario of concept shift. First, we observe on
Figure

8(c)

that for all compressors the convergence rate remains in
O(1/K)
, (though vanilla SGD converges faster during the first iterations). Second, we observe that control-variates improve convergence for compressors inducing un-structured noise ; this is predicted by theory, see
Theorem

S4
.
Third, on
Figure

8(f)
, at each iteration
k\in[K]
, we use deterministic gradients
g_{k}^{i}=\nabla F_{i}
which leads to having a.s.
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=0
, and in the absence of compression, we obtain a
O(1/K^{2})
convergence rate for
\overline{w}_{K}
which corresponds in
Theorem

1
to the case where the dependency on the initial condition is dominated by
\frac{\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|^{2}}{\gamma^{2}K^{2}}
. Overall, these experiments illustrate and support our theoretical insights.

5
Conclusion and open directions

Conclusion.
In short, we investigate the impact of the choice of compression scheme on the convergence of the Polyak-Ruppert averaged iterate. By analysing the case of compressed least-squares regression, we shed light on the interplay between the Hessian of the optimization problem
H_{F}
, the features’ distribution, the additive noise’s covariance
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
, and the compression scheme. This shows fundamental differences between compression that deemed equivalent under the classical worst-case-variance assumption. We extend our analysis to the case of heterogeneous federated learning, a setting in which compression is widely used and its impact not fully understood.

More precisely, first, the analysis of the generic stochastic approximation algorithm (
LSA
) provides (1) the fact that projection based compressions achieve a faster convergence rate than quantization based, and that yet, their asymptotic rate is similar; (2) the analysis of quantization-based compression requires introducing a new Hölder-type regularity assumption for the analysis of the stochastic approximation scheme, and showing that such an assumption is satisfied for quantization.

Second, the computation of the additive noise’s covariance
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
reveals the impact of the compression scheme and the data distribution on the asymptotically dominant term. We obtain that (1) partial participation (i.e., client sampling in the federated case) is the only method that systematically ensures a convergence without a dependency on the strong-convexity constant; (2) other compressors may all induce an un-structured noise, with covariance scaling with
\mathrm{I}
or
\sqrt{H}
, that strongly hinders convergence by accumulating noise on low curvature directions; (3) the relative performance or various schemes changes depending on the pre-processing applied to the data, making quantization the best method (apart from PP) when standardization is applied, but one of the worst (with random Gaussian projection) when the features are independent and the eigenvalues of the covariance decay rapidly (4) in that particular last setting, all projection based methods (but Gaussian projection) behave similarly.

Third, we discuss how these results apply to the federated case, that corresponds to the initial motivation. We show that we encompass two particular heterogeneity situations and how our analysis applies.
Overall, these results are a step towards a better understanding of the impact of a widely used tool.

Open directions.
This analysis can be extended to include various aspects that are beyond the scope of this work. First, one natural improvement for application in FL would be to consider also the scenario where each client runs several
local iterations

(
McMahan et al. 2017
;
Karimireddy et al. 2020
)
before sending their updates, reducing further the cost of communication.
Similar approach can be used, although the additive noise field would be more complicated, which potentially implies a different additive noise’s covariance.
Second, as mentioned in
Section

3.5
, our analysis could also be extended to the case of stochastic approximation with ridge regularization
(
Dieuleveut et al. 2017
, e.g., following )
which in practice is helpful to mitigate the impact of the lack of strong convexity.
Third, an obvious direction is to extend beyond quadratic functions and considering other objective functions, such as logistic regression or even shallow neural networks. Several results in the literature can be leveraged to tackle non quadratic but self-concordant losses
Bach 2010
;
Gadat and Panloup 2023
.
Fourth, our analysis still only relies on second moments (variance and covariance) of the stochastic field. One major drawback of partial participation is to induce a significant increase on higher order moments. Incorporating higher order bounds may also bring novel insights to the use of compression in FL.
Finally, all our analysis is made in finite dimension and our asymptotic focuses on
K\to\infty
: further works should analyze the case of infinite dimension: within the reproducing kernel Hilbert space
(
Dieuleveut and Bach 2016
)
framework or within the overparametrized setting
(
Belkin et al. 2019
)
.

Additional notations.

We use the Frobenius norm
\|A\|^{2}:=\mathrm{Tr}\left(A^{\top}A\right)
, which is the same notation as the vector Euclidean norm (no ambiguity in general),
J_{r}
to denote the
d\times d
diagonal matrix whose
r
first diagonal elements are equal to one and all the other matrix’s coefficients equal to zero,
\mathcal{S}^{++}_{d}(\mathbb{R})
the cone of positive definite symmetric matrices, and
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
the set of
random vectors defined on the probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
such that
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{p}]<\infty
. We define also the operator norm
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}:=\sqrt{\max\mathrm{eig}(A^{\top}A)}
.

A.2
Useful identities and inequalities

In this Subsection, we recall some classical inequalities and results.

Inequality 1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. For any sequence of vector
(a_{i})_{i=1}^{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have the following inequalities:

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}\right\|^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|a_{i}\right\|\right)^{2}\leq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|a_{i}\right\|^{2}\,.

The first part of the inequality corresponds to the triangular inequality, while the second part is Cauchy’s inequality.

Inequality 2
.

Let
x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
A
in
\mathcal{M}_{d,d}(\mathbb{R})
, then we have
\|Ax\|\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\|x\|
.

Below, we recall Minkowski’s and Jensen’s inequalities. Additionally, we recall the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for conditional expectations.

Let a probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
with
\Omega
a sample space,
\mathcal{A}
a
\sigma
-algebra, and
\mathbb{P}
a probability measure.

Minkowski’s inequality.

Let
p>1
and suppose that
X,Y
are two random variables in
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
(i.e. their
p^{\mathrm{th}}
moment is bounded), we have the following triangular inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|X+Y\|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{p}]^{1/p}+\mathbb{E}[\|Y\|^{p}]^{1/p}\,.

(S1)

Jensen’s inequality.

Suppose that
X:\Omega\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a random variable, then for any convex function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
we have:

\displaystyle f\left(\mathbb{E}(X)\right)\leq\mathbb{E}f(X)\,.

(S2)

Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality for conditional expectations.

Suppose that
X,Y
are two random variables in
\mathcal{L}^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
(i.e. their second moment is bounded), then for any
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{A}
we have a.s.:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[XY\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[X^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]\,.

(S3)

Convergence in
L^{p}
-norm.

Suppose that
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of random variables in
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
, and that
X
is a random variable in
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
. We say that
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
L^{p}
-norm towards
X
if
\mathbb{E}(\|X_{n}-X\|^{p})\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}0
, it is denoted by:
X_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{L^{p}}X\,.

In
Section

3.3
, we use ellipses to visual quadratic functions, therefore we provide in
Definition

S1
the mathematical definition.

Definition S1

(Representing positive matrices through ellipsoids)
.

Any symmetric positive definite matrix
M
in
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{++}(\mathbb{R})
defines an ellipsoid

\mathcal{E}_{M}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},x^{\top}M^{-1}x=1\}

centered around zero. The eigenvectors of
M
are the principal axes of the ellipsoid, and the squared root of the eigenvalues are the half-lengths of these axes.
The ellipse corresponds to the sphere of radius 1 associated with the norm
N_{M^{-1}}=\sqrt{x^{\top}M^{-1}x}
.

A.3
An auxiliary inequality

In this Section, we provide an auxiliary lemma that is specific to the framework considered in
Section

2
. It will be used in the proof of
Theorem

1
and corresponds to an adaptation of Lemma 1 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
.

Lemma S1

(Auxiliary inequality on
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k}\|^{2}]/K
)
.

Under
1
and
2.1
, for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any step-size
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
s.t.
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
, the sequence
(w_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
produced by a setting such as in
Definition

1
, verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(w_{k}-w_{*})\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}}{\displaystyle 2\gamma K(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2}))}+\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\,.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we start writing that by
Definition

1
, we have
w_{k}=w_{k-1}-\gamma\nabla F(w_{k-1})+\gamma\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})
. Thus taking the squared norm and developing it, gives:

\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\eta_{k-1}\right\|^{2}-2\gamma\left\langle\eta_{k-1},\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\rangle+\gamma^{2}\left\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\|^{2}\,.

(S4)

We need to bound the last term. By
Definition

2
, we have
\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})=\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)+\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
, hence using
1
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}+2\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}\,,

taking expectation w.r.t the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, developping
\left\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\right\|^{2}
and because
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=0
(the random fields
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are zero-centered, see
Definition

1
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

Now, we use
Definition

1
and
1
and
2.1
: it leads to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq 2R_{F}^{2}\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}+4\mathcal{M}_{2}\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}+8\mathcal{A}+2\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq 2(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}+10\mathcal{A}\,.

Because the sequence of random field
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is zero-centered (
Definition

1
), we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\eta_{k-1},\nabla F(w_{k-1})-\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\right\rangle\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\left\langle\eta_{k-1},H_{F}\eta_{k-1}\right\rangle=\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}\,,

hence back to
Equation

S4
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\eta_{k}\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]&\leq\left\|\eta_{k-1}\right\|^{2}-2\gamma(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2}))\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}+10\mathcal{A}\gamma^{2}\,.\end{split}

(S5)

It follows that if
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
, summing the previous bound and taking full expectation gives:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\|\eta_{K}\right\|^{2}}{\displaystyle 2\gamma K(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2}))}+\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\,,

which allows concluding.

A.4
Asymptotic results: central limit theorem for (
LSA
)

The demonstration of
Proposition

1
uses the following theorem from
Polyak and Juditsky 1992
guaranteeing the asymptotic normality of the Polyak-Ruppert iterate.

Theorem S1
.

From
Polyak and Juditsky 1992
.

For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we denote
\eta_{k}=w_{k}-w_{*}
and we define
w_{k}=w_{k-1}-\gamma_{k}\nabla F(w_{k-1})+\gamma_{k}\xi(\eta_{k-1})
.
If we assume that:

•

\gamma_{k}\xrightarrow[k\rightarrow+\infty]{}0
and
\gamma_{k}^{-1}(\gamma_{k}-\gamma_{k+1})=\underset{k\to+\infty}{o}(\gamma_{k})
,

•

F
is strongly convex and
\left\|\nabla^{2}F\right\|_{\infty}<\infty
,

•

the convergence in probability of the conditional covariance to a matrix
\Sigma
holds, i.e., we have a.s.
\mathbb{E}[\xi(\eta_{k-1})\xi(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\xrightarrow[k\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathbb{P}}\Sigma\,.

Then for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have the asymptotic normality of
(\sqrt{K}\eta_{K-1})_{K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
:

\sqrt{K}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\xrightarrow[K\rightarrow+\infty]{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma^{*})\text{ with }\Sigma^{\star}=\left\{\nabla^{2}F\left(w_{*}\right)\right\}^{-1}\Sigma\left\{\nabla^{2}F\left(w_{*}\right)\right\}^{-1}.

Below we present our CLT that gives the asymptotic normality of
(\sqrt{K}\eta_{K-1})_{K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
in the case of strongly-convex case and decreasing step size.

Proposition S1

(CLT for (
LSA
) in the strongly convex-case and deacreasing step-size)
.

Under
1
and
2
, consider a sequence
(w_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
produced in the setting of
Definition

1
using a step-size
(\gamma_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
s.t.
\gamma_{k}=k^{-\alpha}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then
(\eta_{K})_{K\geq 0}
converges in
L^{2}
-norm to 0, i.e.
\eta_{K}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{L^{2}}0
.

Furthermore,
(\sqrt{K}\overline{\eta}_{K-1})_{K\geq 0}
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and covariance such that:

\sqrt{K}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{N}(0,H_{F}^{-1}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}).

Proof

First, we have that in the case of decreasing step size s.t. for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma_{k}=k^{-\alpha}
, we have:
\eta_{K}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{L^{2}}0
.
This is a classical computation for SGD with bounded variance (
1
and
2.1
.).
Detailed computations are for instance given in lectures notes of
Bach 2022
, and
Kushner and Yin 2003
. To apply Theorem 1 from
Polyak and Juditsky 1992
, which gives the desired result, it suffices to prove the convergence in probability of the covariance of the noise
\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})
towards
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
, as
k\to\infty
.

In the following, we show that
\underset{k\to+\infty}{\lim}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{=}}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
.

We start writing:

\displaystyle\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}

\displaystyle=(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right))(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right))^{\top}

\displaystyle=(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{{\otimes 2}}-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)^{\top}-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\top}+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\,.

(i) First, from
Definition

2
, it flows that
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
.

(ii) Second, we show that
\mathbb{E}[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
converges to
0
in probability: it is sufficient to show that:
\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\|_{F}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{}0\,.

To do so, we use the fact that

\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\|_{F}=\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|_{2}^{2},\,

then with
Item

2.2
:

\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq\mathcal{M}_{1}\|H^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|+\mathcal{M}_{2}\|H^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}
. And we have the result as we showed that
\eta_{k-1}\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{2}}0
.

(iii) Third, it remains to show that
\mathbb{E}[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{1}}0
. We use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality’s
S3
for conditional expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\top}\|_{F}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|_{2}\|(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\top}\|_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

The sequence of random vectors
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is i.i.d., and moreover we have shown previously that

\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
tends to 0, hence
\mathbb{E}[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
converges to
0
in probability.
Consequently, we can state that

\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{\mathbb{P}}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\,.

Appendix B
Generalization of Bach and Moulines (2013).

In this section, we give the demonstration of
Theorem

1
which extends Theorem 1 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
; the demonstration is close to the original one.

B.1
Proof principle

For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the proof relies (1) on decomposing
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]
in two terms using the Minkowski inequality
S1
to make appear a recursion
(\eta_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
without multiplicative noise, and another
(\alpha_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
without additive noise, (2) on an expansion of
\eta_{k}^{0}
and
\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0}
as polynomials in
\gamma
, and (3) on using the Hölder-type
Item

2.2
to bound
\alpha_{k}
. We define the sequence
(\eta_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
such that it involves only an additive noise:

\displaystyle\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\,.

(S6)

Then, we decompose
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]
in the following way using Minkowski inequality
S1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right)^{2}\,.

(S7)

The goal is then to establish a bound for the two above quantities.

1. Bounding
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]
.

The bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]
is given in
Lemma

S2
.
For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the proof relies on an expansion of
\eta_{k}^{0}
and
\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0}
as polynomials in
\gamma
. The recursion defining the sequence
(\eta_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
.
If we denote
M_{i}^{k}=\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F}\right)^{k-i}
and
M_{i}^{i-1}=\mathrm{I}_{d}
, we have:

\eta_{k}^{0}=M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}M_{i+1}^{k}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\,.

For
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, it leads to
\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}+\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{K}M_{k+1}^{i}\right)\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
, and with Minkowski inequality
S1
to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{H_{F}^{1/2}}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}\right\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\gamma H_{F}^{1/2}}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{i=k}^{K}M_{k+1}^{i}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(S8)

The left term depends only on initial conditions
\eta_{0}^{0}
(
=\eta_{0}
) and the right term depends only on the additive noise. This is why, in the proof, we expend
\eta_{k-1}^{0}
and
\overline{\eta}_{k-1}^{0}
separately for the noise process (i.e., when assuming
\eta_{0}=0
) and for the noise-free process
that depends only on the initial conditions (i.e. when assuming that the additive noise
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is uniformly equal to zero). In the end, the two bounds computed separately may be added.

2. Bounding
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}]
.

The bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}]
is given in
Lemma

S3
.
For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the demonstration is based on an exact expression of
\alpha_{k}=\eta_{k}-\eta_{k}^{0}
and
\overline{\alpha}_{k}
computed by unrolling the recursion from
\alpha_{k}
to
\alpha_{0}
. Because
\alpha_{0}=0
and because there is no additive noise involved in
\alpha_{k}
, we obtain for
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, an expression of
\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}
that depends only on the multiplicative noise at iteration
k
in
\{1,\cdots,K\}
:

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{K-k})(\gamma H_{F})^{-1}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})\,.

We then show (
Equation

S11
) that bounding
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}]
leads to bound the following sum
\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
, and this bound is established using the Hölder-type
Item

2.2
; which concludes this part of the proof.

Figure S1
:
Proof principle of
Theorem

S2

B.2
Two bounds

In this subsection, we give two lemmas that provide a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}]
. These bounds are required due to the decomposition of
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]
done in
Equation

S7
.

•

The bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0}\|^{2}]
is given in
Lemma

S2
. It is established by decomposing the noise process and the noise-free process. The bound on the noise process comes from Lemma 2
(
Bach and Moulines 2013
)
and involves the additive noise’s covariance
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
.

•

The bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}]
is established in
Lemma

S3
.

Note that in order to demonstrate
Lemma

S3
, we need to bound
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k}\|^{2}/K
. This is done in
Lemma

S1
which is an adaptation of Lemma 1 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
to random mechanisms. This auxiliary lemma holds for any kind of multiplicative noise – linear or non-linear.

Below lemma provides a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0}\|^{2}]
.

Lemma S2

(Bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0}\|^{2}]
)
.

Under the setting considered in
Definition

1
, under
1
, for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any step-size
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
s.t.
\gamma R_{F}^{2}\leq 1
,
the sequence
(\eta_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
defined in
Equation

S6
verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}\right)\,.

Proof

The proof relies on the proof presented by
Bach and Moulines 2013
and is done separately for the noise process and for the noise-free process that depends only on the initial condition. The bounds may then be added (see the discussion in
Section

B.1
).

Noise-free process.

As in section A.3 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
, we assume in this section that the random fields
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is uniformly equal to zero and that
\gamma R_{F}^{2}\leq 1
. We thus have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
that
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}
.

First inequality.

By recursion, we have
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}
, averaging over
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and computing the resulting geometric sum, we have:

\displaystyle\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}=\frac{\displaystyle 1}{\displaystyle K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}=\frac{1}{K}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{K-1})(\gamma H_{F})^{-1}\eta_{0}^{0}\preccurlyeq\frac{\displaystyle 1}{\displaystyle\gamma K}H_{F}^{-1}\eta_{0}^{0}.

And because
\eta_{0}^{0}=\eta_{0}
, it gives
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0},H_{F}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\right\rangle\right]\leq\frac{\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{0}\|^{2}}{\gamma^{2}K^{2}}
.

Second inequality.
From the expression of
\eta_{k}^{0}
flows:

\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k}^{0}\|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k-1}^{0}\|^{2}]-2\gamma\left\langle\eta_{k-1}^{0},H_{F}\eta_{k-1}^{0}\right\rangle+\gamma^{2}\left\langle\eta_{k-1}^{0},H_{F}^{2}\eta_{k-1}^{0}\right\rangle\,.

Considering that
H_{F}\preccurlyeq\mathrm{Tr}\left(H_{F}\right)\mathrm{I}_{d}\preccurlyeq R_{F}^{2}\mathrm{I}_{d}
(
Definition

1
) and that
\gamma R_{F}^{2}\leq 1
,
because
\eta_{0}^{0}=\eta_{0}
, by convexity we have:
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}^{0}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\left\|\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}}{\gamma K}\,.

Putting things together.

In the end, we take the minimum of the two above bounds and obtain that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|^{2}}{\displaystyle\gamma^{2}K^{2}}\wedge\frac{\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}}{\displaystyle\gamma K}\,.

(S9)

Noise process.

We assume in this part that
\eta_{0}^{0}=\eta_{0}=0
. We apply Lemma 2 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
to
\eta_{k-1}^{0}
. This sequence of iterates has an i.i.d. noise process
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
which is such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right]=\penalty\ \mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
(existence guaranteed by
1
). Therefore we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}{\displaystyle K}\,.

(S10)

Putting things together.

We now take results derived from the part without noise and the part with noise, and we get from Minkowski inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}\right)\,.

Below lemma provides a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}]
.

Lemma S3

(Bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}]
)
.

Under the setting considered in
Definition

1
with
\mu>0
, under
1
, under
Items

2.1
and
2.2
, for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any step-size
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
s.t.
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})<1
,
the sequence
(\overline{\eta}_{k}-\overline{\eta}_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 15\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,.

Remark S1
.

To demonstrate
Lemma

S3
, we use the Hölder-type
Item

2.2
. This is why we obtain a term with a square root in the bound.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we denote
\alpha_{k}=\eta_{k}-\eta_{k}^{0}
, with
\eta_{k}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}+\gamma\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})
and
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
. First, we write the exact expression of
\alpha_{k-1}
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\alpha_{k-1}+\gamma(\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k}\alpha_{0}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}(\xi_{i}(\eta_{i-1})-\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}})\,,

and because
\eta_{0}^{0}=\eta_{0}
, it follows that
\alpha_{0}=\eta_{0}-\eta_{0}^{0}=0
. Averaging over
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have the exact expression of
\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}
:

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}(\xi_{i}(\eta_{i-1})-\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K-1}\left(\sum_{k=i}^{K-1}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\right)(\xi_{i}(\eta_{i-1})-\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}))\,.

Computing the geometric sum results in:

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{K-k})(\gamma H_{F})^{-1}(\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})\,.

And because for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}
,
0\preccurlyeq(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k}\preccurlyeq\mathrm{I}_{d}
, we obtain:

\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\preccurlyeq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}H_{F}^{-1}(\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})\,,

hence
\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\|^{2}=\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}H_{F}^{-1/2}(\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})\|^{2}
. We take full expectation, because for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, by
Definitions

1
and
2
,
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)=\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
is
\mathcal{F}_{k}
-measurable and
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=0
, we can unroll the sum and we have in the end that the variance of the sum is the sum of variances:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

(S11)

Computing
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
for
k
in
\mathbb{N}
, we first have:

\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|H_{F}^{-1/2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\,,

where we used
2
. Because
H_{F}
is a symmetric semi-positive matrix, we have
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|H_{F}^{-1/2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}^{2}=1/\mu
, hence:

\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\leq\mu^{-1}\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}
.
Taking expectation conditionally to the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
and invoking
Item

2.2
gives:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|+3\mathcal{M}_{2}\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2})\,.\end{split}

(S12)

Combining equations
S11
and
S12
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{M}_{1}}{\mu K^{2}}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|]+\frac{3\mathcal{M}_{2}}{\mu K^{2}}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]\,.

Now using Jensen’s inequality for concave function allows us to write:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|]\leq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}]}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}]}\,,

thus we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{M}_{1}}{\mu K}\sqrt{\frac{1}{K}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]}+\frac{3\mathcal{M}_{2}}{\mu K^{2}}\sum^{K-1}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]\,.

Using
Lemma

S1
(with
\eta_{0}=0
), we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}}+\frac{\displaystyle 15\mathcal{A}\gamma\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)\,.

In the end, we take the square root (and use that for any
a,b
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
) which allows concluding:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 15\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,.

B.3
Final theorem

In this section, we gather the pieces of proof required to demonstrate
Theorem

1
.

Theorem S2

(Non-linear multiplicative noise)
.

Under
2
and
1
, considering any constant step-size
\gamma
such that
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1/2
, then for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the sequence
(w_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
produced by a setting such as in
Definition

1
verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(\overline{w}_{K-1})-F(w_{*})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2K}\Bigg(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}+\left(10\mathcal{A}\gamma\right)^{1/4}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(30\mathcal{A}\gamma\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\Bigg)^{2}\,.

Proof

As explained in the discussion in
Section

B.1
(
Equation

S7
), we define the sequence
(\eta_{k}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
which involves only an additive noise
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
. Then, we decompose
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|]
using Minkowski’s inequality
S1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right)^{2}\,.

(S13)

First term.

To bound the first term, we use
Lemma

S2
which gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}\right)\,.

Second term.

From
Lemma

S3
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K}-\overline{\eta}_{K}^{0})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\Bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 15\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,.

Final bound.

Hence, back to
Equation

S13
, we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\Bigg(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 5\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\left(\frac{\displaystyle 15\mathcal{A}\gamma}{\displaystyle 1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})}\right)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,,

and considering
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+2\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1/2
, it concludes the proof because
\mathbb{E}[F(\overline{w}_{K-1})-F(w_{*})]=\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]/2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(\overline{w}_{K-1})-F(w_{*})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2K}\Bigg(\frac{\displaystyle\|H_{F}^{-1/2}\eta_{0}\|}{\displaystyle\gamma\sqrt{K}}\wedge\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}+\left(10\mathcal{A}\gamma\right)^{1/4}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}\mu^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(30\mathcal{A}\gamma\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{2}\mu^{-1}}\Bigg)^{2}\,.

Appendix C
Generalisation of Bach and Moulines (2013) for linear multiplicative noise.

In this Section, we give the demonstration of
Theorem

2
which extends Theorem 1 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
to the case of linear multiplicative noise. The demonstration follows the same steps as the one given by
Bach and Moulines 2013
. The minor differences lie in the generality of the form of the multiplicative noise in our approach.
Bach and Moulines 2013
only analyse LMS algorithm, while we here consider (
LSA
) with assumptions on the linear multiplicative noise process. Moreover, our theorem decomposes into 3 terms instead of 2.

C.1
Proof principle

For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the proof relies on an expansion of
\eta_{k}
and
\overline{\eta}_{k}
as polynomials in
\gamma
. Because we consider a linear multiplicative noise, there exists a matrix
\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t. for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(z)=\Xi_{k}z
(
3
); hence the recursion defined in
Definition

1
can be rewritten as:

\eta_{k}=\eta_{k-1}-\gamma\nabla F(\eta_{k-1})+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F}+\gamma\Xi_{k})\eta_{k-1}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\,.

We denote
M_{i}^{k}=\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F}+\gamma\Xi_{k}\right)\cdots\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F}+\gamma\Xi_{i}\right)
and
M_{i}^{i-1}=\mathrm{I}_{d}
, then we have that
\eta_{k}=M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}M_{i+1}^{k}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}.

For
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, it leads to
\overline{\eta}_{K-1}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\sum_{i=k}^{K}M_{k+1}^{i}\right)\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
, and with Minkowski’s inequality
S1
to:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\right\|^{2}\right]}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{H_{F}^{1/2}}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}M_{1}^{k}\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\gamma H_{F}^{1/2}}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{i=k}^{K}M_{k+1}^{i}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(S14)

The left term depends only on initial conditions and the right term depends only on the noise process.

This is why, in the proof, we expend
\eta_{k-1}
and
\overline{\eta}_{k-1}
separately for the noise process (i.e., when assuming
\eta_{0}=0
) and for the noise-free process
that depends only on the initial conditions (i.e. when assuming that the additive noise
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is uniformly equal to zero). In the end, the two bounds computed separately may be added.

To study the noise process, inspiring from
Bach and Moulines 2013
, we define the following sequence:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\\
&\eta_{k}^{r}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{r}+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r-1}\right)\qquad\text{with}\qquad\forall r\geq 0\,,\eta_{0}^{r}=0\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(S15)

Then, we decompose
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]
in the following way using Minkowski’s inequality
S1
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}\right]}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i})\|^{2}]^{1/2}.

The goal is then to establish a bound for the two above quantities.

Figure S2
:
Proof principle of
Theorem

S3
.

C.2
Lemmas for the noise process

In this Subsection, we provide lemmas for the noise process, and thus we suppose that
\eta_{0}=0
. The noise-free process is later considered in
Section

C.3
and puts together with the results of the coming Subsection. The sketch of the proof relies on establishing two bounds.

•

For
r,k
in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{*}
, noting
\alpha_{k}^{r}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
, the first one is a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r}\|^{2}]
that tends to zero when
r
tends to
+\infty
.

•

The second one is on
\sum_{i=0}^{r}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]
and is established using Lemma 2 from
(
Bach and Moulines 2013
)
. It will correspond to the final variance term and it involves the additive noise’s covariance
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
.

In the following, we provide
Lemmas

S6
,
S5
and
S4
. Let
r,k
in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

•

Lemma

S4
builds a recursive expression of
\alpha_{k}^{r}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
.

•

Lemma

S5
provides a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r}\|^{2}]
which involves
\mathbb{E}\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}
.

•

Lemma

S6
bounds the covariance of
\eta_{k-1}^{r}
, this result will be necessary when computing
the expectation of
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}
.

Below, we provide the lemma that builds a recursive expression of
\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
, with
k,r
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

Lemma S4

(A recursion on
\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
)
.

Under the setting given in
Definition

1
, considering that
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\cdot\right)
is linear (
3
), for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any step-size
\gamma>0
, considering
(\eta_{k}^{r})_{r\in\mathbb{N}}
as given by
Equation

S15
, denoting for
r
in
\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha_{k}^{r}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
, we have the following recursive expression for the sequence of iterate
(\alpha_{k}^{r})_{r\in\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle\forall r\geq 0,\alpha_{k}^{r}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\alpha_{k-1}^{r}+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\,.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the proof is done by recursion. For
r=0
, by
Definitions

1
and
2
, we have
\eta_{k}=\eta_{k-1}-\gamma\nabla F(w_{k-1})+\gamma\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)
, which gives:

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{0}=\eta_{k}-\eta_{k}^{0}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)\bigg\}-\bigg\{(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})(\eta_{k-1}-\eta_{k-1}^{0})+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})(\eta_{k-1}-\eta_{k-1}^{0})+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}-\eta_{k-1}^{0}\right)+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{0}\right)\,,

which is possible because
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}
is linear (
3
).
To go from
r
to
r+1
, we have
\alpha_{k}^{r+1}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r+1}\eta_{k}^{i}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}-\eta_{k}^{r+1}
.
Then by definition of
\eta_{k}^{r+1}
and using the hypothesis:

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{r+1}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\left(\eta_{k-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k-1}^{i}\right)+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k-1}^{i}\right)+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{r+1}-\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\left(\eta_{k-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r+1}\eta_{k-1}^{i}\right)+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r+1}\eta_{k-1}^{i}\right)+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r+1}\right)\,,

again by linearity. This concludes the proof.

The next lemma is the adaptation to our settings of Lemma 1 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
. We give a bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r}\|^{2}]
with a quantity that tends to
0
. This result will be used in the final demonstration of
Theorem

S3
.

Lemma S5

(Bound on
\eta_{K}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{K}^{i}
)
.

Under the setting given in
Definition

1
, considering that
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}
is linear (
3
), for any
r,K
in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any step-size
\gamma
s.t.
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
, the recursion
\alpha_{K}^{r}=\eta_{K}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{K}^{i}
verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\forall r\geq 0,(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))\mathbb{E}\left\langle\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r},H_{F}\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r}\right\rangle\leq\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\,.

Proof

Let
r,k
in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we denote
\alpha_{k}^{r}=\eta_{k}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\eta_{k}^{i}
, then we have shown in
Lemma

S4
that:

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{r}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\alpha_{k-1}^{r}+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\,.

Taking the squared norm and developing it:

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{k}^{r}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\|^{2}+2\gamma\left\langle\alpha_{k-1}^{r},\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)-H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma^{2}\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)-H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\|^{2}\,,

and developing the last term with
1
leads to:

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{k}^{r}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\|^{2}+2\gamma\left\langle\alpha_{k-1}^{r},\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)+\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)-H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+2\gamma^{2}\bigg\{\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}+\|H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\bigg\}\,.

Because
\alpha_{k-1}^{r}
is
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
-measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=0
(expectation of
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\cdot\right)
is zero, see
Definitions

2
and
1
), taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, using
3
and again
Definition

1
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}-\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\leq(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\|H_{F}^{1/2}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\|^{2}\,.

Hence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\alpha_{k}^{r}\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\|\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\|^{2}-2\gamma(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))\left\langle\alpha_{k-1}^{r},H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+2\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\,,

which gives when taking full expectation and averaging over
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
:

\displaystyle(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left\langle\alpha_{k-1}^{r},H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\gamma}(\left\|\alpha_{0}^{r}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\|^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\gamma}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}]\,,

and by convexity
\left\langle\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r},H\overline{\alpha}_{K-1}^{r}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\langle\alpha_{k-1}^{r},H_{F}\alpha_{k-1}^{r}\right\rangle
, which allows to conclude as
\alpha_{0}^{r}=0
.

In below lemma, we bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right]
for
r,k
in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{*}
. It is required because we will use Lemma 2 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
and apply it to the sequence
(\eta_{k-1}^{r})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},r\in\mathbb{N}}
. The noise process of this sequence is equal to
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r-1}\right)
; and computing the expectation of its covariance involves knowing
\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right]
.

Lemma S6

(Bounding the covariance of
\eta_{k-1}^{r}
)
.

Under the setting in
Definition

1
, under
3
,
4
and
1
, i.e. considering that
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\cdot\right)
is linear, for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, any step-size
\gamma>0
,
and for any
r\geq 0
, we have the following bound on the covariance of
\eta_{k-1}^{r}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right]\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+1}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r}\mathrm{I}_{d}\,.

Proof

Let
r>0
, we first prove by recursion that we have:

\forall k>0\,,\eta_{k}^{r+1}=\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r})\,.

For
k=0
, we indeed have
\eta^{r+1}_{0}=0
.
To go from
k
to
k+1
:

\displaystyle\eta_{k+1}^{r+1}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta^{r+1}_{k}+\gamma\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k+1}(\eta_{k}^{r})\qquad\text{by definition,}

\displaystyle=\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r})+\gamma(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{(k+1)-(k+1)}\xi_{k+1}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k}^{r})\,,

by hypothesis, which allows concluding.

We now prove by recursion the main result of the lemma.

Initialization.

For
r=0
, by definition, we have
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
, unrolling the sum gives
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k}\eta_{0}^{0}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}
. Because we consider
\eta_{0}^{0}=0
and given that the sequence of noise
(\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}})_{i\in\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket}
is independent at each iterations, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{0}\otimes\eta_{k}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}\right](\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\,.

Because the sequence of additive noise
(\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is i.i.d., for any
i
in
\{1,\cdots,k\}
, we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{i}^{\mathrm{add}}\right]=\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\preccurlyeq\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}H_{F}
(
Item

4.1
), hence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{0}\otimes\eta_{k}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}H_{F}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\,.

These matrices commute:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{0}\otimes\eta_{k}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k-2i}H_{F}\,,\penalty\ \text{and because it is a geometric sum:}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k-2}\right)\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2}\right)^{-1}H_{F}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k-2}\right)\left(2\gamma H_{F}-\gamma^{2}H_{F}^{2}\right)^{-1}H_{F}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}H_{F}^{-1}H_{F}\qquad\text{because $\gamma H_{F}\preccurlyeq\mathrm{I}_{d}$,}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\mathrm{I}_{d}\,.

Recursion.
Let
r\geq 0
, to go from
r
to
r+1
, we start writing:

\displaystyle\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-1-i}\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r})\otimes\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r})(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-1-i}\,.

Now we use linearity of the multiplicative noise (
3
), thus there exists a matrix
\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t. for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(z)=\Xi_{k}z
, and it leads to:

\displaystyle\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\Xi_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{i-1}^{r})\Xi_{i}^{\top}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\,.

Taking full expectation, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{i}(\eta_{i-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{i-1}^{r})\Xi_{i}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\Xi_{i})\right]\right](\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{i}\mathbb{E}[\eta_{i-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{i-1}^{r}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\Xi_{i})]\Xi_{i}^{\top}\right](\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\,,

and because for any
i
in
\{1,\cdots,k\}
,
\eta_{i-1}^{r}
is independent of
\Xi_{i}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{i-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{i-1}^{r}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\Xi_{i})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{i-1}^{r}\otimes\eta_{i-1}^{r}\right]\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+1}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r}\mathrm{I}_{d}
, where we use the hypothesis for
r
.
We have in the end:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}\right]

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+3}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{i}\Xi_{i}^{\top}\right](\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{k-i}\,.

Furthermore, by
Item

4.2
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{i}\Xi_{i}^{\top}\right]\preccurlyeq\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}H_{F}
, thus:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}\right]

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+3}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k-2-2i}H_{F}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+3}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}\gamma^{-1}H_{F}^{-1}H_{F}\,,

because
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k-2-2i}=\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}-(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})^{2k}\right)\left(2\gamma H_{F}-\gamma^{2}H_{F}^{2}\right)^{-1}\preccurlyeq\gamma^{-1}H_{F}^{-1}
.
In the end, we have
\mathbb{E}[\eta_{k}^{r+1}\otimes\eta_{k}^{r+1}]\preccurlyeq\gamma^{r+2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}\mathrm{I}_{d}
,
which concludes the proof.

C.3
Final theorem

In this section, we gather the pieces of proof required to demonstrate
Theorem

2
. As done in
Appendix

B
, we consider separately the noise process and the noise-free process, then put them together to obtain the final result.

Theorem S3

(Linear multiplicative noise, convex case)
.

Under
1
, under
3
and
4
i.e. with a linear multiplicative noise, considering any constant step-size
\gamma
such that
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
and
4\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}R_{F}^{2}\leq 1
, then for any
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the sequence
(w_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
produced by a setting such as in
Definition

1
, verifies the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(\overline{w}_{K-1})-F(w_{*})]\leq\frac{1}{2K}\left(\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}+\frac{\displaystyle\left(\gamma d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}\right)^{1/2}}{\displaystyle 1-\sqrt{\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}}\right)^{2}\,.

Proof

Let
K
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the proof relies on the proof presented by
Bach and Moulines 2013
and is done separately for the noise process and for the noise-free process that depends only on the initial condition. The bounds may then be added (see the discussion in
Section

C.1
).

Noise-free process.

As in section A.3 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
, we assume here that the additive noise
(\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is uniformly equal to zero and that
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
. Using
Definitions

2
and
1
, we thus have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
that
\eta_{k}=\eta_{k-1}-\gamma H_{F}\eta_{k-1}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})
, it flows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]-2\gamma\mathbb{E}[\left\langle\eta_{k-1},H_{F}\eta_{k-1}\right\rangle]+\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}\eta_{k-1}-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]-2\gamma\mathbb{E}[\left\langle\eta_{k-1},H_{F}\eta_{k-1}\right\rangle]+\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]+\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta_{k-1})\|^{2}]\,.

Considering that
H_{F}\preccurlyeq\mathrm{Tr}\left(H_{F}\right)\mathrm{I}_{d}\preccurlyeq R_{F}^{2}\mathrm{I}_{d}
and using
3
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k}\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]-2\gamma\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]+\gamma^{2}(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\eta_{k-1}\|^{2}]\,.

Because the step-size
\gamma
is s.t.
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
,
we recover that in the absence of noise, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\left\|\eta_{0}\right\|^{2}}{\gamma K}\,.

(S16)

Noise process.

Now, all the following results comes from
Section

C.2
where we assume that
\eta_{0}=w_{0}-w_{*}=0
, we start using Minkowski’s inequality
S1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|H_{F}^{1/2}(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i})\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,.

(S17)

First term.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
, again using Minkowski’s inequality
S1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{r}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}
