\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]^{1/2}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}\,.

(S18)

By
Equation

S15
, we have
\eta_{k}^{0}=(\mathrm{I}_{d}-\gamma H_{F})\eta_{k-1}^{0}+\gamma\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
, hence to bound the first term, we have to apply Lemma 2 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
to the sequence
(\eta_{k-1}^{0})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{0}\|^{2}]\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)/K\,.

(S19)

Let
i
in
\{1,\cdots,r\}
, to bound the second term, we have to apply Lemma 2 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
to the sequence
(\eta_{k-1}^{i})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
.
To do so, we bound the covariance of the noise which is here equal to
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)
(by definition of
\eta_{k-1}^{i}
, see
Equation

S15
).

Because the multiplicative noise is linear, using
3
, there exists a matrix
\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t.
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)=\Xi_{k}\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}
.
It follows that taking the expectation w.r.t to the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\Xi_{k})
, and because
\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}
is independent of it, using
Lemma

S6
, we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\Xi_{k})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right]\preccurlyeq\gamma^{i}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{i-1}\mathrm{I}_{d}\,.

Thus, the noise
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)
is such that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)\otimes\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\Xi_{k})]

\displaystyle=\Xi_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\otimes\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right]\Xi_{k}^{\top}\preccurlyeq\gamma^{i}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{i-1}\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\,.

Taking full expectation, we furthermore consider
Item

4.2
which gives that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{i}\Xi_{i}^{\top}\right]\preccurlyeq\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}H_{F}
,
hence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)\otimes\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{i-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{i}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{i}H_{F}\,.

(S20)

Using Lemma 2 from
Bach and Moulines 2013
results to:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r}\gamma^{i}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{i}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H_{F}H_{F}^{-1}\right)/K\,.

(S21)

In the end, we obtain from
Section

C.3
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|H_{F}^{1/2}\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}}{\displaystyle\sqrt{K}}+\frac{\displaystyle\sqrt{d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}}}{\displaystyle\sqrt{K}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\gamma^{i/2}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{i/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}}{\displaystyle\sqrt{K}}+\frac{\sqrt{\gamma d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}\left(1-(\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}})^{r/2}\right)}{\sqrt{K}\left(1-\sqrt{\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}\right)}\,.

Second term.

If
\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2})\leq 1
,
Lemma

S5
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i},H(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i})\right\rangle\leq\frac{\gamma}{(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}\right]\,.

(S22)

Furthermore,
\left\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\right\|^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)^{{\otimes 2}}\right)
, by reusing what has been written in the previous paragraph (
Equation

S20
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta_{k-1}^{r}\right)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{r+1}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H_{F}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{r+1}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}R_{F}^{2}\qquad\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:class_of_algo}).}

It follows that we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i},H(\overline{\eta}_{K-1}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}^{i})\right\rangle\leq\frac{\gamma^{r+2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}R_{F}^{2}}{(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))}\,.

(S23)

Putting things together.

In the end, from the Minkowski decomposition done in
Equation

S17
, we combine the two terms and it leads to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1},H_{F}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\right\rangle\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\gamma^{r+2}\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}^{r+1}R_{F}^{2}}{(1-\gamma(R_{F}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{2}))}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}}{\displaystyle\sqrt{K}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{\gamma d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}\left(1-(\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}})^{r/2}\right)}{\sqrt{K}\left(1-\sqrt{\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}\right)}\,.

This implies that for any
\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}\leq 1
, we obtain, by letting
r
tend to
+\infty
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1},H_{F}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\right\rangle\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle 1}{\displaystyle\sqrt{K}}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}+\frac{\displaystyle\left(\gamma d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}\right)^{1/2}}{\displaystyle 1-\sqrt{\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}}\right)\,.

(S24)

Final bound.

We now take results derived from the part without noise, and the part with noise, to get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left\langle\overline{\eta}_{K-1},H_{F}\overline{\eta}_{K-1}\right\rangle]^{1/2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}\left(\frac{\|\eta_{0}\|}{\sqrt{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}H_{F}^{-1}\right)}+\frac{\displaystyle\left(\gamma d\Sha_{\mathrm{add}}\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}\right)^{1/2}}{\displaystyle 1-\sqrt{\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}}}\right)\,,

which leads to the desired result considering that
4\gamma\Sha_{\mathrm{mult}}\leq 1
.

Appendix D
Validity of the assumptions made on the random fields

In this section, we verify that all the assumptions on the random fields done in
Section

2.1
are fulfilled in the setting of compressed least-squares regression analyzed in
Section

3
.
To do so, we first need to define the filtrations considered in this section.

For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
u_{k}
the noise that controls the randomization
\mathcal{C}_{k}(\cdot)
at round
k
.
In
Section

2
, we have denoted by
\mathcal{F}_{k}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
(x_{1},\varepsilon_{1},u_{1},\cdots,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k})
. In particular,
w_{k}
and
\overline{w}_{k}
are
\mathcal{F}_{k}
-measurable.
We also consider the following filtrations.

Definition S2
.

We note
(\mathcal{G}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated with the features noise,
(\mathcal{H}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated with the output noise, and
(\mathcal{I}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated with the stochastic gradient noise, which is the union of the two previous filtrations. Thus, we define
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\{\varnothing\}
and for
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{x_{k}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{\varepsilon_{k}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k},u_{k}\}\right)\,.

Note that there are two filtrations
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{H}
for the two independent noises that are both involved to compute the stochastic gradient. This will help us to compute the scalar product of these two quantities.

We start by providing a bound on the distance between two compressions, this lemma will be used to prove
S3
.

Lemma S7
.

For any compressor
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathbb{C}
verifying
Lemma

1
, for all
x,y
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\left\|\mathcal{C}(x)-\mathcal{C}(y)\right\|^{2}]\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|x\right\|^{2}+2(\omega+1)\left\|y\right\|^{2}\,.

Proof

Let a compressor
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathbb{C}
and
x,y
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, using
1
, we have that:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{C}(x)-\mathcal{C}(y)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\mathcal{C}(x)\right\|^{2}+2\left\|\mathcal{C}(y)\right\|^{2}\,.

Taking expectation and using
Lemma

1
allows to conclude:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{C}(x)-\mathcal{C}(y)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|x\right\|^{2}+2(\omega+1)\left\|y\right\|^{2}\,.

Now we prove that all the assumptions done in
Section

2
are correct.

Property S1

(Validity of the setting presented in
Definition

1
)
.

Consider
2
in the context of
2
, we have that the setting presented in
Definition

1
is verified.

Proof

From
2
, we have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}

\xi_{k}(w-w_{*})=\nabla F(w)-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))
.
Because
(\textsl{g}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
and
(\mathcal{C}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are by definition two sequences of i.i.d. random fields (
2
), it follows that their composition is also i.i.d.,
hence
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random fields.

Taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}_{k}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]=\textsl{g}_{k}(w)
(
Lemma

1
), next with the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\nabla F(w)
(Equation
2
).

Hence, the random fields are zero-centered.

From
2
, we have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
that:

\displaystyle F(w)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[(\left\langle x_{k},w\right\rangle-y_{k})^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[(w-w_{*})^{\top}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})(w-w_{*})-2\varepsilon_{k}\left\langle x_{k},w-w_{*}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{k}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}((w-w_{*})^{\top}H(w-w_{*})+\sigma^{2})\,,

hence
F
is quadratic with Hessian equal to
H
whose trace is equal to
R^{2}
.

Property S2

(Validity of
1
)
.

Considering
2
under the setting of
2
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the additive noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
can be bounded by
(\omega+1)R^{2}\sigma^{2}
, i.e.,
1
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Because we consider
2
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we first have
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=-\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})
, then with
Lemma

1
we obtain
\mathbb{E}[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})\right\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}]\leq(\omega+1)\left\|g_{k,*}\right\|^{2}
.
Next, we first have from
2
and
Equation

2
that
g_{k,*}=\varepsilon_{k}x_{k}
, secondly because
\left((\varepsilon_{k})_{k\in[K]}\right)
is independent from
\left((x_{k})_{k\in[K]}\right)
(
2
), we have that
\mathbb{E}[\|\varepsilon_{k}x_{k}\|^{2}]\leq\sigma^{2}R^{2}
, hence it results to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}]\leq(\omega+1)\sigma^{2}R^{2}\,.

Property S3

(Validity of
Item

2.1
)
.

Considering
2
, under the setting of
2
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)
can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
2(\omega+1)R^{2}\left\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}+4(\omega+1)\sigma^{2}R^{2}
, i.e.,
Item

2.1
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
. First, because we consider
2
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we have
\xi_{k}(\eta)=\nabla F(w)-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))
and
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=-\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})
, hence:

\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}(\eta)=\xi_{k}(\eta)-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=\nabla F(w)-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))+\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})\,,

thus developing the squared-norm of
\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}(\eta)
gives:

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}(\eta)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla F(w),\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\,.

On the first side we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=-\nabla F(w_{k-1})
.
On the second side, we use
Lemma

S7
; this allows us to write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\|^{2}+2(\omega+1)\left\|g_{k,*}\right\|^{2}\,.

Note that this bound is far from being optimal when
\textsl{g}_{k}(w)=g_{k,*}
or if
\mathcal{C}
is the identity.
Next, we decompose as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|\textsl{g}_{k}(w)-g_{k,*}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4(\omega+1)\left\langle\textsl{g}_{k}(w)-g_{k,*},g_{k,*}\right\rangle+4(\omega+1)\left\|g_{k,*}\right\|^{2}\,.

Taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{G}_{k}
, recalling that
\textsl{g}_{k}(w)-g_{k,*}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable (
Definition

S2
) and considering
2
allows to write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\|^{2}+4(\omega+1)\sigma^{2}R^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\omega+1)\left\|(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\eta_{k-1}\right\|^{2}+4(\omega+1)\sigma^{2}R^{2}\,,

and now taking expectation w.r.t the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
concludes the proof:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\leq 2(\omega+1)R^{2}\|H^{1/2}(w_{k}-w_{*})\|^{2}+4(\omega+1)\sigma^{2}R^{2}\,.

Property S4

(Validity of
Item

2.2
)
.

Considering
2
, under the setting of
2
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)
can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
\Omega R^{2}\sigma\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|+3(\omega+1)R^{2}\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}
, i.e.
Item

2.2
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
. Because we consider
2
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla F(w),\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\,.

We take expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}_{k}
and use
Item

L.2
of
Lemma

1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla F(w),g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+\Omega\min(\|g_{k,*}\|,\|\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|)\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|+3(\omega+1)\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|^{2}\,.

Then, we have
\min(\|g_{k,*}\|,\|\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|)\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|\leq\|g_{k,*}\|\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|
, taking expectation conditionally to the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz’s
Equation

S3
and considering
2
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|g_{k,*}\|\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|g_{k,*}\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\mathbb{E}[\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}R^{4}\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\,.

Therefore, we can conclude:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+\sigma R^{2}\Omega\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|+3(\omega+1)R^{2}\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\,.

Property S5

(Validity of
3
)
.

Considering
2
, under the setting of
2
with
Lemma

1
, if the compressor
\mathcal{C}
is linear, then for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}
is linear, thus there exist a matrix
\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)=\Xi_{k}w
. Furthermore, the second moment of the multiplicative noise can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
(\omega+1)R^{2}\left\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}
, hence
3
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
. First, because we consider
2
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we have
\xi_{k}(\eta)=\nabla F(w)-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))
and
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=-\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})
, hence:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)=\xi_{k}(\eta)-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=\nabla F(w)-\mathcal{C}_{k}(\textsl{g}_{k}(w))+\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*})\,.

Because the random mechanism
\mathcal{C}_{k}
is linear, there exists a random matrix
\Pi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{k}(z)=\Pi_{k}z
, it follows that:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)=\nabla F(w)+\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w))=(H-\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k}))\eta\,.

Hence, the first part of
3
is verified with
\Xi_{k}=H-\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})
. Now, we compute the second moment of the multiplicative noise. We start by developing its squared norm:

\displaystyle\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla F(w),\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\rangle+\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}(g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w))\right\|^{2}\,.

Taking expectation conditionally to the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}_{k}
, and using
Lemma

1
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right))\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\nabla F(w),g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\rangle+(\omega+1)\left\|g_{k,*}-\textsl{g}_{k}(w)\right\|^{2}\,.

Finally, with
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
and considering
2
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=-\left\|\nabla F(w)\right\|^{2}+(\omega+1)R^{2}\|H^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\,,

which allows to conclude.

Property S6

(Validity of
4
)
.

Considering
2
under the setting of
2
with
Remarks

1
and
1
, if the compressor
\mathcal{C}
is linear, then
for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, there exists a constant
\Sha_{H}>0
s.t.
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\preccurlyeq\sigma^{2}\Sha_{H}H_{F}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\right]\preccurlyeq R^{2}\Sha_{H}H
;
4
is thus verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
.
We first need to compute
\Sha_{H}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
for each compressor
\mathcal{C}
in
\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sq}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}1},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
, it comes from
Proposition

2
which results having a constant
\Sha_{H}
s.t.:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C},p_{{H}})=\mathbb{E}_{E\sim p_{H}}[\mathcal{C}(E)^{{\otimes 2}}]\preccurlyeq\Sha_{H}H\,.

(S25)

Indeed,
\mathrm{Diag}\left(H\right)
car be bounded by
\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\right)\mathrm{I}_{d}
, and then
\mathrm{I}_{d}
by
\mu^{-1}H
. This constant
\Sha_{H}
can be computed from
Proposition

2
for any compressor:

Compressor

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h}

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}

\Sha_{H}

\frac{h-1}{p(d-1)}+(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1})\frac{\tau}{p}

1+\frac{(1-p)\tau}{p}

\frac{1}{p}

\frac{\alpha-\beta}{p}+\frac{\beta\tau}{p}

\Sha_{H}
(if
H
diagonal)

\frac{1}{p}

\frac{1}{p}

\frac{1}{p}

\frac{\alpha-\beta}{p}+\frac{\beta\tau}{p}

Where
p=h/d
,
\tau=\mathrm{Tr}\left(H\right)/\mu
, and for sketching
\alpha=\frac{h+2}{d+2}
and
\beta=\frac{d-h}{(d-1)(d+2)}
.

We now show that the two inequalities given in
4
are valid.

First inequality.

By
Definition

3
, we have
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}=\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}(\varepsilon_{k}x_{k})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]
, because
\left((\varepsilon_{k})_{k\in[K]}\right)
is independent from
\left((x_{k})_{k\in[K]}\right)
(
2
) and using compressor linearity and
Equation

S25
, it gives:
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}=\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}(x_{k})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]=\sigma^{2}\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C},p_{{H}})\preccurlyeq\sigma^{2}\Sha_{H}H\,.

Second inequality.

Using
S6
, because the compressor
\mathcal{C}
is linear, there exists two matrices
\Pi_{k},\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t. for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{k}(z)=\Pi_{k}z
and
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(z)=\Xi_{k}z
, which gives that
\Xi_{k}=H-\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})
.
It follows that:

\displaystyle\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}=HH^{\top}-H\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})-\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})H+\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\Pi_{k}^{\top}\,.

Given that the compression is unbiased (
Lemma

1
) we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{k}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]=\mathrm{I}_{d}
, hence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=HH^{\top}-H(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})-(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})H+\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\Pi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]\,,

and now taking expectation w.r.t the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=-HH^{\top}+\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\Pi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

In the end, we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\preccurlyeq\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\Pi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]
,
and if we consider that the second moment of the features
(x_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is almost surely bounded (
Remark

1
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq R^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{k}(x_{k}\otimes x_{k})\Pi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\preccurlyeq R^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}(x_{k})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

(S26)

Thus, using
Equation

S25
, we can state that
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\preccurlyeq R^{2}\Sha_{H}H
,
which concludes the second part of the verification of
4
.

Appendix E
Compression operators

In this Section, we provide additional details about compression operators. First, we prove in
Section

E.1
that
Lemma

1
hold and compute the compressor’s covariance given in
Proposition

2
. The specific computations for sketching are given separately in
Section

E.2
because they are more complex. Third, it allows to prove
Propositions

3
and
4
in
Section

E.3
. And finally, in
Section

E.4
, we plot the covariance matrix induced by quantization and sparsification for
quantum
and
cifar-10
.

E.1
Computation of the variance and covariance of the compression operators

In this Subsection, we first prove
Lemma

1
.
Item

L.1
is frequently established in the literature and corresponds to the worst-case assumption, see the introduction for references. On the other hand,
Item

L.2
is the Hölder-type bound, which is not used in the literature up to our knowledge. Next, we compute the compressors’ covariances that have been given in
Proposition

2
.

Lemma S8
.

For any compressor
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sq}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}\}
, there exists constants
\omega,\Omega\in\mathbb{R}^{*}_{+}
, such that the random operator
\mathcal{C}
satisfies the following properties for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

L.1:

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}(z)]=z
and
\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{C}(z)-z\|^{2}]\leq\omega\|z\|^{2}
(unbiasedness and variance relatively bounded),

L.2:

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{C}(z)-\mathcal{C}(z^{\prime})\|^{2}]\leq\Omega\min(\|z\|,\|z^{\prime}\|)\|z-z^{\prime}\|+3(\omega+1)\|z-z^{\prime}\|^{2}\text{(Hölder-type bound),}

with
\omega=\sqrt{d}
and
\Omega=12\sqrt{d}
(resp.
\omega=(1-p)/p
and
\Omega=0
) for
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{sq}}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}}
).

Proof

Value of
\omega
(
Item

L.1
of
Lemma

1
).

For projection-based compressors, the proof is straightforward, for quantization-based, the proof can be found in
Alistarh et al. 2017
.

Value of
\Omega
(
Item

L.2
of
Lemma

1
).

For linear compressors, it is straightforward to obtain
\Omega=0
.

For quantization, we take
x,y
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we note
(u_{i})_{i=1}^{d}
the vector controlling the randomness of compression, and we write
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}(x)-\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}(y)=A+B+C
, with:

1.

A:=\|x\|\mathrm{sign}(x)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|x|}{\|x\|})-\|x\|\mathrm{sign}(x)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|x|}{\|y\|})

2.

B:=\|x\|\mathrm{sign}(x)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|x|}{\|y\|})-\|x\|\mathrm{sign}(y)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|y|}{\|y\|})

3.

C:=\|x\|\mathrm{sign}(y)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|y|}{\|y\|})-\|y\|\mathrm{sign}(y)\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|y|}{\|y\|})
.

We note
\|\cdot\|
the 2-norm and
\|\cdot\|_{1}
the 1-norm. By symmetry, we suppose that
\|y\|^{2}\geq\|x\|^{2}
.

First term.
We have
\|A\|^{2}=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|x\|}}-\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}})^{2}=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{1}_{\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}\leq u_{i}\leq\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|x\|}}^{2}
because
\|y\|^{2}\geq\|x\|^{2}
. Taking expectation, it gives
\mathbb{E}[\|A\|^{2}]=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|x\|}-\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}=\|x\|^{2}\|x\|_{1}\frac{\|y\|-\|x\|}{\|y\|\|x\|}
. Now with triangular inequality, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\|A\|^{2}]\leq\frac{\|x\|}{\|y\|}\|x\|_{1}\|y-x\|\leq\|x\|_{1}\|y-x\|\leq\sqrt{d}\|x\|\|y-x\|\,,

and by symmetry
\mathbb{E}[\|A\|^{2}]\leq\sqrt{d}\min(\|x\|,\|y\|)\|y-x\|
.

Second term.

We have
\|B\|^{2}=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}}-\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|y_{i}|}{\|y\|}})^{2}
. Let
i
in
[d]
, if
\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})=\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})
, then:

\mathbb{E}\left[\|B\|^{2}\right]=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{1}_{\frac{\min(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)}{\|y\|}\leq u_{i}\leq\frac{\max(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)}{\|y\|}}^{2}\right]=\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{\|y\|}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|y_{i}-x_{i}|\leq\|x\|\|x-y\|_{1}\,.

If
\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\neq\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})
, developping
(\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}}-\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|y_{i}|}{\|y\|}})^{2}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|B\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\|x\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{|x_{i}|}{\|y\|}+\frac{|y_{i}|}{\|y\|}-2\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})\frac{\min(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)}{\|y\|}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{\|y\|}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)+3\min(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)\,.

Next, we have
\max(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)+\min(|x_{i}|,|y_{i}|)=|x_{i}|+|y_{i}|\overset{\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\neq\mathrm{sign}(y_{i})}{=}|x_{i}-y_{i}|
, which results to
\mathbb{E}\left[\|B\|^{2}\right]\leq 3\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{\|y\|}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|y_{i}-x_{i}|\leq 3\|x\|\|x-y\|_{1}\leq 3\sqrt{d}\|x\|\|x-y\|
.

Third term.
We have
\|C\|^{2}=(\|x\|-\|y\|)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{1}_{u_{i}\leq\frac{|y_{i}|}{\|y\|}}^{2}
, taking expectation, it gives:

\mathbb{E}[\|C\|^{2}]=(\|x\|-\|y\|)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{|y_{i}|}{\|y\|}\leq\|x-y\|^{2}\frac{\|y\|_{1}}{\|y\|}\leq\sqrt{d}\|x-y\|^{2}\,.

Overall
, using
1
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}(x)-\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}}(y)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 12\sqrt{d}\min(\|x\|,\|y\|)\|x-y\|+3\sqrt{d}\|x-y\|^{2}\,,

which allows to conclude as for
1
-quantization, we have
\omega=\sqrt{d}
.

We now compute the compressors’ covariance given in
Propositions

2
and
3
. However, sketching requires more involved computations, they are provided in
Section

E.2
.

Proposition S2

(Structure of the compressor’s covariance)
.

The following formulas of compressors’ covariance hold:

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\emptyset},p_{{M}})=M

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{q},p_{{M}})\preccurlyeq M+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}\sqrt{\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)}-\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{s},p_{{M}})=M+\frac{1-p}{p}\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},p_{{M}})=\frac{1}{p}\left((\alpha-\beta)M+\beta\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)
with
\alpha=\frac{h+2}{d+2}
and
\beta=\frac{d-h}{(d-1)(d+2)}

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})=\frac{d(h-1)}{h(d-1)}M+\left(\frac{d}{h}-\frac{d(h-1)}{h(d-1)}\right)\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=\frac{1}{p}M
.

Proof

In this proof, we denote
\mathcal{F}
the
\sigma
-field generated by the random sampling of
E\sim p_{M}\in\mathcal{P}_{M}
, and
\mathcal{G}
the
\sigma
-field generated by the noise from the compression process.
Let
E\sim p_{M}\in\mathcal{P}_{M}
.

Quantization.

By definition, we have
\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)=\|E\|_{2}\mathrm{sign}(E)\odot\chi
, with
\chi=\left(\mathrm{Bern}(\frac{|E_{i}|}{\|E\|_{2}})\right)_{i=1}^{d}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)^{\otimes 2}=\|E\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{sign}(E)^{\otimes 2}\odot\chi^{\otimes 2}
.

Because:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\chi^{\otimes 2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\displaystyle|E_{i}|}{\displaystyle\|E\|_{2}}\quad\text{if}\quad i=j\\
\frac{\displaystyle|E_{i}|\penalty\ |E_{j}|}{\displaystyle\|E\|_{2}^{2}}\quad\text{else,}\end{array}\right.

and considering that
\mathrm{sign}(E)^{\otimes 2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&\mathrm{sign}(E_{i})\mathrm{sign}(E_{j})\\
&\ddots&\\
\mathrm{sign}(E_{i})\mathrm{sign}(E_{j})&&1\end{pmatrix}
,
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)^{\otimes 2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\|E\|_{2}\penalty\ |E_{i}|\quad\text{if}\quad i=j\,,\\
E_{i}E_{j}\quad\text{else.}\end{array}\right.

Taking the complete expectation gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)^{\otimes 2}\right]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{E}\left[\|E\|_{2}\penalty\ |E_{i}|\right]\quad\text{if}\quad i=j\\
M_{ij}\quad\text{else.}\end{array}\right.

Changing the diagonal to make appear
M
, we obtain:

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)^{\otimes 2}\right]=M+\mathbb{E}\left[\|E\|_{2}\mathrm{Diag}\left(|E_{i}|\right)_{i=1}^{d}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Diag}\left(E_{i}^{2}\right)_{i=1}^{d}\right]\,.

Furthermore, we first have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Diag}\left(E_{i}^{2}\right)_{i=1}^{d}\right]=\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)
and secondly, by Cauchy-Schwarz
Equation

S3
that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\|E\|_{2}\mathrm{Diag}\left(|E_{i}|\right)_{i=1}^{d}\right]^{2}\preccurlyeq\mathbb{E}\left[\|E\|_{2}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Diag}\left(E_{i}^{2}\right)_{i=1}^{d}\right]=\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)\,,

which finally gives
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{q}(E)^{\otimes 2}\right]\preccurlyeq M+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}\sqrt{\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)}-\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)\,.

Sparsification.

By definition, we have
\mathcal{C}_{s}(E)=\frac{1}{p}B\odot E\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with
B\sim\left(\text{Bern}(p)\right)_{i=1}^{d}
, thus
\mathcal{C}_{s}(E)^{\otimes 2}=\frac{1}{p^{2}}B^{\otimes 2}\odot E^{\otimes 2}
.
Taking the expectation w.r.t. to the
\sigma
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{s}(E)^{\otimes 2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=\frac{1}{p^{2}}P\odot E^{\otimes 2}
with
P=\begin{pmatrix}p&&p^{2}\\
&\ddots&\\
p^{2}&&p\end{pmatrix}\,,
because for all
i,j
in
\llbracket 1,d\rrbracket
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=p
and
\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i}B_{j}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=p^{2}
. This naturally gives:
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{s}(E)^{\otimes 2}\right]=\frac{1}{p^{2}}P\odot M
.

Sketching.

The proof is more complex and therefore is given separately, in
Section

E.2.3
.

Rand-
h
.

By definition, we have
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h}(E):=\frac{d}{h}B(S)\odot E
with
S\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{P}_{h}([d]))
and
B(S)_{i}=\mathbb{1}_{i\in S}
, thus
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h}(E)^{\otimes 2}=\frac{1}{p^{2}}B^{\otimes 2}\odot E^{\otimes 2}
(
p=h/d
).
We have that for any
i,j
in
\{1,\dots,d\}
,
B_{i}
and
B_{j}
are
not
independent and that
B_{i}\sim\left(\text{Bern}(p)\right)
, therefore we have that
\mathbb{E}[B_{i}^{2}]=p
and that:
h^{2}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}B_{i}\right)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}B_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}B_{i}B_{j}
. Taking expectation, it gives
h^{2}=h+d(d-1)\mathbb{E}[B_{i}B_{j}]
i.e.
\mathbb{E}[B_{i}B_{j}]=\frac{h(h-1)}{d(d-1)}
.
Taking the expectation w.r.t. to the
\sigma
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
, we have :

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h}(E)^{\otimes 2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}\right]=\frac{d(h-1)}{h(d-1)}E^{{\otimes 2}}+\left(\frac{d}{h}-\frac{d(h-1)}{h(d-1)}\right)\mathrm{Diag}\left(E^{{\otimes 2}}\right)\,.

And taking full expectation allows conclusion.

Partial Participation.
This result is straightforward.

E.2
Variance and covariance of sketching

In this Subsection, we compute the expectation, the variance, and the covariance of sketching. In
Section

E.2.1
, we give the proof principle of our computation, in
Section

E.2.2
, we compute the expectation and the variance, and in
Section

E.2.3
, we compute the covariance.

We thank Baptiste Goujaud (École polytechnique, CMAP) who greatly helped to prove the following.

E.2.1
Proof principle

Let
y
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\|y\right\|^{2}=1
, and
x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By
Definition

4
, for
\Phi
in
\mathbb{R}^{h\times d}
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}(x)=\frac{1}{p}\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi x
with
\Phi^{\dagger}=\Phi^{\top}(\Phi\Phi^{T})^{-1}
and
p=h/d
.

To compute the expectation, the variance, and the covariance of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}(x)
, the idea is to compute
\mathbb{E}[y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)]
and
\mathbb{E}[(y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x))^{2}]
by establishing
Equation

S27
which allows controlling the randomness of sketching by using
Equation

S28
.
To establish
Equation

S27
, first observe that
pC_{\Phi}(\cdots)
is a projector into a subspace of dimension
h
, indeed we have
(pC_{\Phi}\odot pC_{\Phi})(x)=pC_{\Phi}(x)
. Then there exists a random matrix
P
in
\mathcal{O}_{d}
s.t.
pC_{\Phi}(x)=P^{\top}J_{h}Px
. It leads to:

\displaystyle y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}y^{\top}P^{\top}J_{h}Px=\frac{1}{p}(Py)^{\top}J_{h}(Px)\,.

Now we note
X=Px/\|x\|
and
Y=Py
, hence
y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)=\frac{\|x\|}{p}Y^{\top}J_{h}X
, and because
P
is in
\mathcal{O}_{d}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|X\right\|^{2}=1\\
\left\|Y\right\|^{2}=\left\|y\right\|^{2}=1\\
\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle=\left\langle x,y\right\rangle/\|x\|\,.\end{array}\right.

Furthermore,
P
is a random projector, it follows that
X
and
Y
are sampled uniformly from the zero-center sphere of radius 1; i.e.
X\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{S}_{d}(0,1))
and
Y\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{S}_{d}(0,1))
. However,
X
and
Y
are not independent, this is why, we consider that
X\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{S}_{d}(0,1))
and write
Y
s.t.
Y=aX+bu
with
u
a random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
of norm
1
orthogonal to
X
, that is to say,
u|X
is uniformly sampled on a zero-centered hyper-sphere of radius
1
orthogonal to the vector
X
(see illustration on
Figure

S3
). It comes that:

\displaystyle y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)=\frac{\|x\|}{p}Y^{\top}J_{h}X=\frac{\|x\|}{p}(aX^{\top}+bu^{T})J_{h}X=\frac{\|x\|}{p}(aX^{\top}J_{h}X+bu^{\top}J_{h}X)\,.

(S27)

Observe that for any
i,j
in
\{1,\cdots,d\}
,
X_{i}
,
X_{j}
(resp.
u_{i}
,
u_{j}
) have the same law, it results to:

\displaystyle\forall(i,j)\in\{1,\cdots,d\}^{2},\penalty\ \forall k\in\mathbb{N},\qquad\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{k}]=\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{k}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}[u_{i}^{k}]=\mathbb{E}[u_{j}^{k}]\,.

(S28)

This property is the key to compute the expectation, the variance, and the covariance of sketching.

Figure S3
:
Sphere zero-center with radius 1:
X
and
u
are orthogonal.

We now compute
a
and
b
. First, by definition, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle}{\|x\|}=\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle=a\left\|X\right\|^{2}=a\,,

then we write that:

\displaystyle 1=\left\|Y\right\|^{2}=\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{4}}\left\|X\right\|^{2}+b^{2}\left\|u\right\|^{2}=\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{2}}+b^{2}\,,

which gives
b=\sqrt{1-\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{2}}}
.

At the end, we have:
Y=aX+bu=\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle}{\|x\|}X+\sqrt{1-\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{2}}}u
.

E.2.2
Expectation and variance of sketching

In this Subsection, we prove that sketching verifies
Item

L.1
in
Lemma

1
; for this purpose, we show that it is unbiased, then we compute its variance.

Proposition S3
.

Sketching is unbiased and its variance is relatively bounded, i.e., it verifies
Item

L.1
in
Lemma

1
with
\omega=(1-p)/p
where
p=h/d
.

Proof

Starting from
Equation

S27
, we have
y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)=\frac{\|x\|}{p}(aX^{\top}J_{h}X+bu^{\top}J_{h}X)
. We first compute the expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\{X\})
generated by the noise involved in the random vector
X
, it gives:

\mathbb{E}[y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]=\frac{\|x\|}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{h}aX_{i}^{2}+bX_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]\,.

Because
u
is sampled uniformly from the zero-center sphere of radius
1
s.t. it is orthogonal to
X
, for any
i
in
\{1,\cdots,d\}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[u_{i}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]=0
, hence taking full expectation, we obtain:

\mathbb{E}[y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)]=\frac{\|x\|}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{h}a\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]\,.

Using
Equation

S28
, we have
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{2}]
, next recalling that
p=h/d
and
\|X\|^{2}=1
, it leads to
\mathbb{E}[y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)]=a\|x\|\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j=1}^{d}X_{j}^{2}]=a\|x\|\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{2}]=a\|x\|
. And because
a=\left\langle x,y\right\rangle/\|x\|
, we have at the end that
\mathbb{E}[C_{\Phi}(x)]=x
.
Now we compute the variance:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[C_{\Phi}(x)^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)]=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\mathbb{E}[x^{\top}P^{\top}J_{h}PP^{\top}J_{h}Px]=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\mathbb{E}[x^{\top}P^{\top}J_{h}Px]=\frac{\left\|x\right\|^{2}}{p^{2}}\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}J_{h}X]\,.

\mathbb{E}[X^{\top}J_{h}X]
has been computed above and is equal to
p
, it results that
\mathbb{E}[C_{\Phi}(x)^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x)]=\left\|x\right\|^{2}/p
.
In the end, sketching verifies
Lemma

1
with
\omega=(1-p)/p
.

E.2.3
Covariance of sketching.

In this Subsection, we compute the covariance of sketching. For the sake of demonstration, we need to compute the
4^{\mathrm{th}}
-moment of
X_{1}
and the
2^{\mathrm{nd}}
-moment of
u_{1}
. For any
i
in
[d]
and any vector
v
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we note
v_{-i}=(v_{j})_{j\in[d],j\neq i}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
.

Computing the
4^{\mathrm{th}}
-moment of
X_{1}
.

The marginal density of
X_{1}
is
f_{X_{1}}:x\mapsto B(\frac{d-1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{-1}(1-x^{2})^{(d-3)/2}
where
B
is the beta function defined as
B:x,y\mapsto\int_{0}^{1}t^{x-1}(1-t)^{y-1}=2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\sin^{2x-1}(t)\cos^{2y-1}(t)\mathrm{d}t
. This result can be obtained either by an application of the formula for the surface area of a sphere
(
Li 2010
;
Sidiropoulos 2014
)
, either by writing that
X_{1}=\frac{Z_{1}}{\|Z\|}
with
Z
a Gaussian vector with
d
components.
Therefore we have that:

\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]=\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}x^{4}(1-x^{2})^{(d-3)/2}\mathrm{d}x}{2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\sin^{d-2}(t)\mathrm{d}t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\cos^{4}(t)\sin^{d-2}(t)\mathrm{d}t}{2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\sin^{d-2}(t)\mathrm{d}t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{=}}\frac{W_{d-2}-2W_{d}+W_{d+2}}{W_{d-2}}\,,

where at (i) we set
x=\cos(t)
and at (ii) we make appears the Wallis’ integrals defined for any
n
in
\mathbb{N}
as
W_{n}=\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\sin^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t
. Furthermore, we have the following recursion using integration by parts:
W_{d+2}=\frac{d+1}{d+2}W_{d}
, therefore, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]=\left(1-\frac{2(d-1)}{d}+\frac{(d-1)(d+1)}{d(d+2)}\right)=\frac{3}{d(d+2)}\,.

(S29)

Computing the
2^{\mathrm{nd}}
-moment of
u_{1}
w.r.t the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma({X})
.

Figure S4
:
Parallel hyperplanes
P
and
P^{\prime}
with the sphera
S
.

We define three
(d-2)
–dimensional manifolds, two parallel hyperplanes
P,P^{\prime}
and a sphere
S
, as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}P=\{\tilde{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\mid\langle\tilde{u},X_{-i}\rangle=-X_{i}u_{i}\}\\
P^{\prime}=\{\tilde{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\mid\langle\tilde{u},X_{-i}\rangle=0\}\\
S=S_{d-1}(0,\sqrt{1-u_{1}^{2}})\end{array}\right.

Obviously
u_{-i}
is in
P\cap S
; then we decompose
u_{-i}
in two terms
n+v
, with
v\sim\mathrm{Unif}(P^{\prime})
orthogonal to
X
and independent of
u_{i}
:
n
is the center of the sphere
S\cap P
and
v
is its radius,
n
corresponds also to the normal vector of both
P,P^{\prime}
with norm equal to the distance between the two hyperplanes, hence
n=\frac{\langle u_{-i},X_{-i}\rangle}{\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}X_{-i}=-\frac{u_{i}X_{i}}{\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}X_{-i}
.

First, because
u_{-1}\in S
, we have
\|n+v\|^{2}=1-u_{1}^{2}
, next by Pythagorean theorem this is equivalent to
\|v\|^{2}=1-u_{1}^{2}-\|n\|^{2}=1-\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{\|X_{-1}\|^{2}}
. Second, because
u_{-1}\in P
, we have
u_{1}=\frac{-\langle u_{-1},X_{-1}\rangle}{X_{1}}
, that is to say the probability density function of
u_{1}\mid X
is proportional to the number of possible values for
u_{-1}
, which corresponds to the surface area of the hypersphere
P\cap S
. This surface is proportional to the radius
\|v\|^{d-4}=(1-\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{\|X_{-1}\|^{2}})^{(d-4)/2}
given that
P\cap S
is a
(d-3)
–dimensional manifold, therefore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]=\frac{\int_{-\|X_{-1}\|}^{\|X_{-1}\|}x^{2}\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}\right)^{(d-4)/2}\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{-\|X_{-1}\|}^{\|X_{-1}\|}\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}\right)^{(d-4)/2}\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{\|X_{-1}\|^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}y^{2}\left(1-y^{2}\right)^{(d-4)/2}\mathrm{d}y}{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(1-y^{2}\right)^{(d-4)/2}\mathrm{d}y}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{=}}\|X_{-1}\|^{2}\frac{W_{d-3}-W_{d-1}}{W_{d-3}}\,,

where at (i) we set
y=\frac{x}{\|X_{-1}\|}
and at (ii) we reuse the previous computations to make appear the Wallis’ integral. In the end, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]=(1-\frac{d-2}{d-1})\|X_{-1}\|^{2}=\frac{\|X_{-1}\|^{2}}{d-1}\,.

(S30)

Note that this result is consistent with the fact that
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[u_{i}^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]=\frac{d-\sum_{i=1}^{d}X_{i}^{2}}{d-1}=1
. Now we can compute the covariance of the sketching operator.

Proposition S4
.

Let
x
in
p_{M}
, the covariance of sketching is equal to:

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}(x)^{{\otimes 2}}]=\frac{1}{p}((\alpha-\beta)M+\beta\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{I}_{d})\,,\vskip-14.22636pt

with
\alpha=\frac{h+2}{d+2}
and
\beta=\frac{d-h}{(d-1)(d+2)}
.

Proof

Let
x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
y
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\|y\right\|^{2}=1
, starting from
Equation

S27
, we have:

\displaystyle(y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x))^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{p^{2}}(aX^{\top}J_{h}X+bu^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{p^{2}}\Bigg(a^{2}(X^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}+2ab(X^{\top}J_{h}Xu^{\top}J_{h}X)+b^{2}(u^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}\Bigg)\,.

First term.
Taking expectation, we have
\mathbb{E}[(X^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}]=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]+\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2}]\right)
.
However:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}X_{j}^{2}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{k=1,k\neq i}^{d}X_{k}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{=}}\frac{h-1}{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}(1-X_{i}^{2})\right]\,,

where we use at line (i)
Equation

S28
and at line (ii)
\sum_{i=1}^{d}X_{i}^{2}=1
. It follows that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(X^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(\frac{d-h}{d-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]+\frac{h-1}{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{h(d-h)}{d-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]+\frac{h-1}{d-1}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(iii)}}}{{=}}\frac{h(d-h)}{d-1}\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]+\frac{h(h-1)}{d(d-1)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{eq.\penalty\ \ref{app:eq:fourth_moment_X1}}}}{{=}}\frac{3h(d-h)}{d(d-1)(d+2)}+\frac{h(h-1)}{d(d-1)}=\frac{h(h+2)}{d(d+2)}:=\alpha^{\prime}\,.

Where we considered at line (i) that for any
i
in
\{1,\cdots,h\}
,
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]=\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]
, and at line (ii) that
\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]=\frac{h}{d}\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{2}]=h/d
.

Second term.
We compute the expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\{X\})
generated by the noise involved in the random vector
X
. It gives
\mathbb{E}\left[X^{\top}J_{h}Xu^{\top}J_{h}X\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]=0
, because
u|X
is uniformly sampled on a zero-centered hyper-sphere, and thus for any
i
in
\{1,\cdots,d\}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[u_{i}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})]\penalty\ =\penalty\ 0
.

Third term.
We have
(u^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{h}u_{i}^{2}X_{i}^{2}+\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}u_{i}u_{j}X_{i}X_{j}
.
On one side, we compute the expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\{X\})
generated by the noise involved in the random vector
X
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}^{2}X_{i}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}X_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{eq.\penalty\ \ref{app:eq:second_moment_u1}}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{i=1}^{h}X_{i}^{2}\|X_{-i}\|^{2}\,.

Taking full expectation, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}[u_{i}^{2}X_{i}^{2}]=\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}(1-X_{i}^{2})]=\frac{h}{d-1}(\frac{1}{d}-\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}])
, because for any
i
in
\{1,\dots,h\}
,
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]=\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]
and
\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]=\frac{h}{d}\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{2}]=h/d
.

Let
i
in
[d]
, on the other side, we compute the expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\{X,u_{i}\})
generated by the noise involved in the random vector
X
and the random variable
u_{i}
, hence we requires to compute
\mathbb{E}\left[u_{j}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X,u_{i}\})\right]
. To do so, as before, we decompose
u_{-i}
in two terms
n+v
(see
Figure

S4
), with
v\sim\mathrm{Unif}(P^{\prime})
orthogonal to
X
and independent of
u_{i}
, hence
\mathbb{E}\left[v\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X,u_{i}\})\right]=0
.
It gives that
\mathbb{E}\left[u_{-i}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X,u_{i}\})\right]=-\frac{u_{i}X_{i}}{\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}X_{-i}
. Thereby, replacing for any coordinate
j\neq i
in
[d]
the value of
u_{-i}
and taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\sigma(\{X\})
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}X_{i}X_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}u_{j}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}\frac{\displaystyle 1}{\displaystyle\|X_{-i}\|^{2}}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \sigma(\{X\})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{eq.\penalty\ \ref{app:eq:second_moment_u1}}}}{{=}}-\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}X_{i}^{2}X_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{d-1}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}X_{i}^{2}\frac{1-X_{i}^{2}}{d-1}\,.

Finally, we have:
\sum_{i=1}^{h}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{h}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}X_{j}u_{i}u_{j}]=-\frac{h(h-1)}{d(d-1)^{2}}(1-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}])
. Putting together the two terms, we have that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(u^{\top}J_{h}X)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{h}{d-1}(\frac{1}{d}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}])-\frac{h(h-1)}{d(d-1)^{2}}(1-d\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{eq.\penalty\ \ref{app:eq:fourth_moment_X1}}}}{{=}}\frac{h(d-h)}{d(d-1)(d+2)}:=\beta^{\prime}\,.

In the end,
we have
\mathbb{E}[(y^{\top}C_{\Phi}(x))^{2}]=\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{p^{2}}(a^{2}\alpha^{\prime}+b^{2}\beta^{\prime})
.
And because
\|y\|^{2}=1
,
a=\left\langle x,y\right\rangle/\|x\|
and
b=\sqrt{1-\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}/\|x\|^{2}}
, replacing them by their values gives:

\displaystyle y^{\top}\mathbb{E}[C_{\Phi}(x))^{{\otimes 2}}]y

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\|x\right\|^{2}}{p^{2}}\left(\alpha^{\prime}\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{2}}+\beta^{\prime}\left(y^{\top}y-\frac{\left\langle x,y\right\rangle^{2}}{\|x\|^{2}}\right)\right)\,,

hence
\mathbb{E}[C_{\Phi}(x))^{{\otimes 2}}]=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left((\alpha^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})xx^{\top}+\beta^{\prime}\left\|x\right\|^{2}\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)
.
To conclude, we consider that
x
is a random variable sampled from a distribution
p_{M}
, then taking expectation on this random variable we have:

\mathbb{E}C_{\Phi}(x)^{{\otimes 2}}=\frac{1}{p}\left((\alpha-\beta)M+\beta\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)
, with
\alpha=\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{p}=\frac{h+2}{d+2}
and
\beta=\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{p}=\frac{d-h}{(d-1)(d+2)}
.

E.3
Proof of Propositions
3
and
4

In this Subsection, we give the proof of
Propositions

3
and
4
which provides generic comparisons between the asymptotic convergence rate of compressors.
We first give a lemma resulting from the Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality necessary to establish these proofs.

Lemma S9

(Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality on matrices’ traces)
.

For any matrix
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we have
\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)\penalty\ \geq\penalty\ d^{2}
, with strict inequalities if
M
is not proportional to
\mathrm{I}_{d}
. And if
M
is with constant diagonal equal to
c
in
\mathbb{R}
, we have
c\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)\geq d
.

Proof

Let
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have:

d^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)^{2}=\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{1/2}M^{-1/2}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{C.S}}}{{\leq}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)\,,

and we have equality if
M
is proportional to
\mathrm{I}_{d}
.

Now we give the demonstration of
Propositions

3
and
4
. On
Figure

S5
, we complete the numerical illustration provided in
Section

3.3.1
by illustrating the scenario of standardized features, i.e., when the diagonal of
M
is the identity.

Figure S5
:
H
not diagonal, scenario using features standardization. Scatter plot of
(x_{k})_{i=1}^{K}
/
(\mathcal{C}(x_{k}))_{i=1}^{K}
with its ellipse
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Cov}\left[x_{k}\right]}
/
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Cov}\left[\mathcal{C}(x_{k})\right]}
.

Proposition S5

(Comparison between
{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}}
,
\omega=d/h-1
)
.

We consider
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}\}
with
p=h/d
, such that
\mathcal{C}
always satisfies
Lemma

1
with
\omega=d/h-1
. For any matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
:

1.

If
M
is diagonal, then:

•

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})=\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})=\frac{d}{h}M
,

•

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}/\mathrm{s}/\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)
.

2.

Moreover, for any matrix
M
with a
constant diagonal
(e.g., after standardization), we have:

\mathrm{Tr}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1})\leq\mathrm{Tr}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},p_{{M}})M^{-1})\leq\mathrm{Tr}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{s},p_{{M}})M^{-1})\leq\mathrm{Tr}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})M^{-1})\,.

With strict inequalities if
M
is not proportional to
\mathrm{I}_{d}
.

Proof

Let
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and take
p=h/d
.

Proof of
Item

1
in
Proposition

3
.

In the diagonal case, the first equalities are straightforward as we have
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})=\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}1},p_{{M}})=\frac{d}{h}M
.
Next, we have (regardless if
M
is diagonal or not):

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{\Phi}},p_{{M}})-\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})\right)M^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=(\frac{h+1}{d+2}+\delta_{hd}-1)\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)}{p}+(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1})\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)}{p(d+2)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{app:lem:cs_matrix}}}}{{\geq}}\frac{d}{p}\left(\frac{h+1}{d+2}+\delta_{hd}-1+\frac{d}{d+2}(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1})\right)

\displaystyle=0\,.

Proof of
Item

2
in
Proposition

3
.

Suppose now that
\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)=c\mathrm{I}_{d}
, then we have
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=\frac{d}{h}M
,
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})=M+(\frac{d}{h}-1)c\mathrm{I}_{d}
,
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})=\frac{d(h-1)}{h(d-1)}M+\frac{d}{h}(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1})c\mathrm{I}_{d}
and
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},p_{{M}})=\frac{d}{h}\left((\frac{h+1}{d+2}-\delta_{hd})M+\left(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1}\right)\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}{d+2}\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)
.
Firstly, from previous item, we have

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{\Phi}},p_{{M}})\right)M^{-1}\,.

Secondly, we write:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\Phi},p_{{M}})-\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})\right)M^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{p}(\frac{h+1}{d+2}+\delta_{hd}-\frac{h}{d})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{c\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)}{p}\left(\frac{d}{d+2}(1-\frac{h-1}{d-1})-(1-\frac{h}{d})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{p}(\frac{h+1}{d+2}+\delta_{hd}-\frac{h}{d})-\frac{c\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)}{p}\cdot\frac{(d-2)(d-h)}{d(d-1)(d+2)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{app:lem:cs_matrix}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{d}{p}\left(\frac{h+1}{d+2}+\delta_{hd}-\frac{h}{d}-\frac{(d-2)(d-h)}{d(d-1)(d+2)}\right)=0\,.

Thirdly, we have:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{rd}h},p_{{M}})-\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})\right)M^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{h-d}{h(d-1)}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)+\frac{d-h}{h(d-1)}c\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{app:lem:cs_matrix}}}}{{\geq}}\frac{d}{h}\left(\frac{h-d}{d-1}+\frac{d-h}{d-1}\right)=0\,.

Proposition S6

(Comparison between
{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}}
,
\omega=\sqrt{d}
)
.

We consider
\mathcal{C}\in\{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}}\}
with
p=(\sqrt{d}+1)^{-1}
, such that
\mathcal{C}
always satisfies
Lemma

1
with
\omega=\sqrt{d}
.

1.

For any symmetric matrix
M
diagonal, we have:

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{s},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)\overset{\text{possib. }\ll}{\leq}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)M^{-1}\right)\,.

2.

If
M
is not necessarily diagonal but with a
constant diagonal
(e.g., after standardization), then

•

\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)\preccurlyeq\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})

•

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)M^{-1}\right)
.

Proof

Let
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and take
p=\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{d}}
.

Proof of
Item

1
in
Proposition

4
.

In the diagonal case with
p=\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{d}}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)=\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}\sqrt{M}
and
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=(1+\sqrt{d})M
, hence
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)M^{-1}\right)=\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\sqrt{M^{-1}}\right)
and
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)=(1+\sqrt{d})d
. Noting
(\lambda_{i})_{i\in[d]}
the eigenvalues of
M
, and using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality’s, we have:

\displaystyle d^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}1\right)^{2}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{1/4}\lambda_{i}^{-1/4}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{C.S}}}{{\leq}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{1/2}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{C.S}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{-1/2}\right)=\sqrt{d\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)}\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1/2}\right)=\sqrt{d}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)M^{-1}\right)\,.

Which follows that
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)M^{-1}\right)\geq d^{3/2}=\sqrt{d}(1+\sqrt{d})^{-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})M^{-1}\right)
and it allows to conclude.

Proof of
Item

2
in
Proposition

4
.

Suppose now that
\mathrm{Diag}\left(M\right)=c\mathrm{I}_{d}
, then we have
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=(\sqrt{d}+1)M
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)=M+(\sqrt{d}-1)c\mathrm{I}_{d}
, and
\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})=M+c\sqrt{d}\mathrm{I}_{d}
.
Firstly, it follows that:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{s}},p_{{M}})-\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)=\left(M+\sqrt{d}c\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)-\left(M+(\sqrt{d}-1)c\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)=c\mathrm{I}_{d}\succcurlyeq 0\,,

Secondly, we have
(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)-\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})=-(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})M+(\sqrt{d}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})c\mathrm{I}_{d}
,
which gives:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left((\sqrt{d}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{q}},M)-\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{PP}},p_{{M}})\right)M^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{d}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})c\mathrm{Tr}\left(M^{-1}\right)-(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{I}_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(\sqrt{d}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})d-(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})d\text{\quad(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{app:lem:cs_matrix})}

\displaystyle\geq d(\sqrt{d}-1)\geq 0\,.

And the proof is concluded.

and

and

. For

and

, resulting in a feature mean
100

Looking at the covariance induced by the compressors, we observe that for small

\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}_{M},p_{{H}})M^{-1}
