Appendix F
Technical results on federated learning.

F.1
Validity of the assumptions made on the random fields in the case of covariate-shift

In this Subsection, we examine the setting of federated and compressed LSR under the scenario of covariate-shift (
Section

4.1
). Specifically, we consider the case where for any
i,j
in
\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
, we have heterogeneous covariances, i.e.,
H_{i}\neq H_{j}
, but a unique optimal model i.e.
w_{*}^{i}=w_{*}
.
We verify that all the assumptions on the random fields done in
Section

2.1
are fulfilled in the setting. For this purpose, we redefine the filtration given in
Appendix

D
to align them with the FL setting. For
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and for
i
in
[N]
, we note
u_{k}^{i}
the noise that controls the compression
\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\cdot)
at round
k
.

Definition S3
.

We note
(\mathcal{G}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated with the features noise,
(\mathcal{H}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated with the label noise, and
(\mathcal{I}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
the filtration associated to the stochastic gradient noise, which is the union of the two previous filtrations. For
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we define
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\{\varnothing\}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{(x_{k}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{(\varepsilon_{k}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{(x_{k}^{i},\varepsilon_{k}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}\}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\cup\{(x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}^{i},u_{k}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}\}\right)\,.

Now we prove that all assumptions done in
Section

2
are correct in this setting.

Property S7

(Validity of the setting presented in
Definition

1
)
.

Consider
3
in the context of
1
, we have that the setting presented in
Definition

1
is verified.

Proof

From
3
, we have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\xi_{k}(w-w_{*})=\nabla F(w)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w))
.
Because
(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},i\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket}
and
(\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},i\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket}
are by definition two sequences of i.i.d. random fields (
3
), it follows that their composition is also i.i.d.,
hence
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random fields.

Taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}_{k}
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w))\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]=\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w)
(
Lemma

1
), next with the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w)\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\nabla F_{i}(w)
(
Equation

2
).
And because
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla F_{i}(w)=\nabla F(w)
,
we obtain that the random fields are zero-centered.

From
1
, we have for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
that:

\displaystyle F(w)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[(\left\langle x_{k}^{i},w\right\rangle-y_{k}^{i})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[(w-w_{*})^{\top}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})(w-w_{*})-2\varepsilon_{k}^{i}\left\langle x_{k}^{i},w-w_{*}\right\rangle+(\varepsilon_{k}^{i})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(w-w_{*})^{\top}H_{i}(w-w_{*})+\sigma^{2}=\frac{1}{2}((w-w_{*})^{\top}\overline{H}(w-w_{*})+\sigma^{2})\,.

And we have from
1
:
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\overline{H}\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H_{i}\right)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R_{i}^{2}=:\overline{R}^{2}
,
which concludes the verification.

Property S8

(Validity of
1
)
.

Consider
3
in the context of
1
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the additive noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}
can be bounded by
(\omega+1)\overline{R}^{2}\sigma^{2}/N
i.e.
1
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Because we consider
3
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we first have
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i})
, hence taking expectation w.r.t the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}_{k}
and because the
N
compressions are independent (
3
), using
Lemma

1
, we have that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i})\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{I}_{k}\right]+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle g_{k,*}^{i},g_{k,*}^{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\omega+1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|g_{k,*}^{i}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle g_{k,*}^{i},g_{k,*}^{j}\right\rangle\,.

Next, we first have from
1
and
Equation

2
that for any
i
in
[N]
,
g_{k,*}^{i}=-\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i}
, secondly because
\left((\varepsilon_{k}^{i})_{k\in[K],i\in[N]}\right)
are independent from
\left((x_{k}^{i})_{k\in[K],i\in[N]}\right)
(
1
), we have that
\mathbb{E}[\|\varepsilon_{k}^{i}x_{k}^{i}\|^{2}]\leq\sigma^{2}R_{i}^{2}
, hence it results to
\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\|^{2}]=\frac{\omega+1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sigma^{2}R_{i}^{2}\,.

Property S9

(Validity of
Item

2.1
)
.

Consider
3
in the context of
1
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)
can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
2(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})\left\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}/N+4(\omega+1)\overline{R}^{2}\sigma^{2}/N
i.e.
Item

2.1
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
.
Because we consider
3
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we write
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)
, where
\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)=H_{i}\eta-\mathcal{C}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w))+\mathcal{C}(g_{k,*}^{i})
is the multiplicative noise on client
i
in
[N]
, hence developing the squared norm gives:

\displaystyle\left\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\right\|^{2}=\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta),\xi_{k}^{j,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\right\rangle\,.

Taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, using that the
N
compressions are independent (
3
) and that for any
i
in
[N]
,
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=0
(
Lemma

1
) results to have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\,.

Next, we use the result of
S3
for each client
i
in
[N]
and we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(2(\omega+1)R_{i}^{2}\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}+4(\omega+1)R_{i}^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})}{N}\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}+\frac{4(\omega+1)\overline{R}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{N}\,,

which allows concluding.

Property S10

(Validity of
Item

2.2
)
.

Consider
3
in the context of
1
with
Lemma

1
, for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the second moment of the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)
can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
(\Omega\sigma\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|+(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2})/N
i.e.
Item

2.2
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
.
From
S9
, taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, decomposing the multiplicative noise results to have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\,.

Next we use the result of
S4
for each client
i
in
[N]
and we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\|^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Omega R_{i}^{2}\sigma\sqrt{\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}}+(\omega+1)R_{i}^{2}\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\,.

With Jensen’s inequality
S2
used for concave function:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Omega\sigma\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})}{N}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Omega\sigma\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})}{N}\sqrt{\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{N}(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\|^{2}\,,

which allows concluding.

Property S11

(Validity of
3
)
.

Consider
3
in the context of
1
with
Lemma

1
, if the compressor
\mathcal{C}
is linear, then for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the multiplicative noise
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}
is linear, thus there exist a matrix
\Xi_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(w)=\Xi_{k}w
. Furthermore the second moment of the multiplicative noise can be bounded for any
w
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})\left\|\overline{H}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}/N
, hence
3
is verified.

Proof

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we note
\eta=w-w_{*}
.
Because we consider
3
, with
Definitions

1
and
2
, we write
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)
, where
\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)=H_{i}\eta-\mathcal{C}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w))+\mathcal{C}(g_{k,*}^{i})
is the multiplicative noise on client
i
in
[N]
.
And because for any clients
i
in
\{1,\cdots N\}
the random mechanism
\mathcal{C}^{i}_{k}
is linear, there exists a random matrix
\Pi_{k}^{i}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t. for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(z)=\Pi_{k}^{i}z
, it follows that:

\displaystyle\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)=\nabla F(w)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w))+\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i})=\left(\overline{H}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Pi_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})\right)\eta\,.

Hence, the first part of
Item

2.2
is verified with
\Xi_{k}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}-\Pi_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})
.
From
S9
, taking expectation w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, decomposing the multiplicative noise results to have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}(\eta)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

Next we use the result of
S5
for each client
i
in
[N]
and we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\xi^{\mathrm{mult}}_{k}\left(\eta\right)\right\|^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\omega+1)R_{i}^{2}\left\|H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\omega+1)\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2})}{N^{2}}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}^{1/2}(w-w_{*})\right\|^{2}\,,

which allows concluding.

Property S12

(Validity of
4
)
.

Considering
3
under the setting of
2
with
Remarks

1
and
1
, if the compressor
\mathcal{C}
is linear, then
for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}\preccurlyeq\sigma^{2}\max_{i\in[N]}(\Sha_{H_{i}})\overline{H}/N
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\right]\preccurlyeq\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2}\Sha_{H_{i}})\overline{H}/N
, with
(\Sha_{H_{i}})_{i\in[N]}
given in
Corollary

2
.
Overall,
4
is thus verified.

Proof

First inequality.

By
Definition

3
, we have
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
, because for any client
i
in
[N]

\left((\varepsilon_{k}^{i})_{k\in[K]}\right)
is independent from
\left((x_{k}^{i})_{k\in[K]}\right)
(
1
) and using compressor linearity and
Equation

S25
, it gives:

\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}=\sigma^{2}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i})^{{\otimes 2}}\right]=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{{H}_{i}})\preccurlyeq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Sha_{H_{i}}H\preccurlyeq\frac{\sigma^{2}\max_{i\in[N]}(\Sha_{H_{i}})}{N}\overline{H}\,.

Second inequality.

Using
S11
, because the random mechanism
\mathcal{C}^{i}
is linear, there exists two matrices
\Pi_{k}^{i},\Xi_{k}^{i}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
s.t. for any
z
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(z)=\Pi_{k}^{i}z
and
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{mult,i}}(z)=\Xi_{k}^{i}z=(H_{i}-\Pi_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})z
, which gives that
\Xi_{k}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H_{i}-\Pi_{k}^{i}(x_{k}^{i}\otimes x_{k}^{i})
.
It follows that:

\displaystyle\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\Xi_{k}^{i})(\Xi_{k}^{i})^{\top}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}(\Xi_{k}^{i})(\Xi_{k}^{j})^{\top}\,.

Taking the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, using that the
N
compressions are independent (
3
) and that for any
i
in
[N]
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{i,\mathrm{mult}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=0
(
Lemma

1
) results to have
\mathbb{E}[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[(\Xi_{k}^{i})(\Xi_{k}^{i})^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]
.
Now, we can reuse the computations given in
S6
to obtain
\mathbb{E}\left[(\Xi_{k}^{i})(\Xi_{k}^{i})^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\preccurlyeq R_{i}^{2}\Sha_{H_{i}}H_{i}
.
Therefore, we can state that
\mathbb{E}\left[\Xi_{k}\Xi_{k}^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\preccurlyeq\max_{i\in[N]}(R_{i}^{2}\Sha_{H_{i}})\overline{H}/N
,
which concludes the second part of the verification of
4
.

F.2
Heterogeneous optimal point

In this section, we explore further the scenario of concept-shift by adding a memory mechanism
(
Mishchenko et al. 2019
)
. This mechanism has been shown by
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
to improve the convergence in the case of heterogeneous clients. We give below the updates equation defining the algorithm of distributed compressed LSR with memory.

Algorithm 4

(Distributed compressed LMS with control variates)
.

Each client
i\in[N]
maintains a sequence
(h_{k}^{i})_{i\in[N]}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, observes at any step
k\in[K]
an oracle
\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(\cdot)
on the gradient of the local objective function
F_{i}
and applies an independent random compression mechanism
\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\cdot)
to the difference
\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}-h_{k}^{i}
. And for any step-size
\gamma>0
, any
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the sequence of iterates
(w_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &w_{k}=w_{k-1}-\frac{\gamma}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{k-1})-h_{k-1}^{i})+h_{k-1}^{i}\\
&h_{k}^{i}=h_{k-1}^{i}+\alpha\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{k-1})-h_{k-1}^{i})\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(S31)

with
\alpha=1/2(\omega+1)
.

The counterpart of adding memory is that the random fields are no more identically distributed, thus
Definition

1
is not fulfilled, and results from
Section

2
cannot be applied, especially because
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right]
changes along iterations.
To remedy this problem, we define here the
limit
of the covariance of the additive noise i.e.
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}=\underset{k\to+\infty}{\lim}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\right]
. In the following result, we establish an asymptotic result on the convergence, similar to
Theorem

1
.

Theorem S4

(CLT for concept-shift heterogeneity)
.

Consider
4
under
1
with
\mu>0
and
Lemma

1
, for any step-size
(\gamma_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
s.t.
\gamma_{k}=1/\sqrt{k}
. Then

1.

(\sqrt{K}\overline{\eta}_{K-1})_{K>0}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{N}(0,H_{F}^{-1}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}H_{F}^{-1})
,

2.

\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}=\overline{\mathfrak{C}((\mathcal{C}^{i},p_{\Theta_{i}^{\prime}})_{i=1}^{N})}
, where
p_{\Theta_{i}^{\prime}}
is the distribution of
g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
.

Theorem

S4
shows that when using memory, in the settings of heterogeneous optimal points
(w_{*}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}
, convergence is still impacted by heterogeneity but with a smaller additive noise’s covariance as
\Theta_{i}^{\prime}\prec\Theta_{i}
. In particular, in the case of deterministic gradients (batch case), we case
\Theta_{i}^{\prime}\equiv 0
.
From a technical standpoint, it shows that we recover asymptotically the results stated by
Theorems

1
and
2
in the general setting of i.i.d. random fields
(\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
.
To prove this theorem, we show that the assumptions required by
Theorem

S1
are fulfilled by this framework.

Proof

For the sake of demonstration, we define a Lyapunov function
V_{k}

(
Mishchenko et al. 2019
;
Liu et al. 2020
;
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
, as in)
, with
k
in
\llbracket 1,K\rrbracket
:

V_{k}=\left\|\eta_{k}\right\|^{2}+2\gamma_{k}^{2}C\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|h_{k-1}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right\|^{2}\,,

with
C
in
\mathbb{R}^{*}
being a Lyapunov constant that verifies some conditions given in Theorem S6 in
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
. For any
k
in
\mathbb{N}
, the Lyapunov function is defined combining two terms: (1) the distance from parameter
w_{k}
to the optimal parameter
w_{*}
, (2) for any client
i
in
[N]
, the distance between the memory term
h_{k-1}^{i}
and the true gradient
\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
.

First, we have that in the case of decreasing step size s.t. for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma_{k}=k^{-\alpha}
, we have
\eta_{K}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{L^{2}}0
and
h_{K}^{i}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{L^{2}}\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
.

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, from the demonstration of the
Artemis
algorithm with memory, we have from Theorem S6 in
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
(see page 41-45) that (1) combining Equations (S14) and (S15) in the form (S14)
+2\gamma_{k}^{2}C
(S15), (2) and applying strong-convexity,
allows to obtain Equation (S17) but adapted to decreasing step-size:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V_{k}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-2\gamma_{k}\mu\Box_{k}\right)\left\|w_{k-1}-w_{*}\right\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{k}^{2}C\diamondsuit}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|h_{k-1}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{k}^{2}\sigma\triangle}{N}\,,

with
\Box_{k},\diamondsuit,\triangle
being three constants in
\mathbb{R}
whose exact expression can be found on pages 42-43 in
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
. Furthermore, in the same article, they verify that to obtain a
(1-\gamma_{k}\mu)
convergence, the following condition on
\Box_{k},\diamondsuit,\triangle
are fulfilled for any
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
:
\Box_{k}\leq 1/2
and
\diamondsuit\leq 1-\gamma_{k}\mu
.

These properties are valid under some conditions on the Lyapunov constant
C
, the step-size
\gamma_{k}
, and the learning rate
\alpha
; these conditions are provided in the statement of Theorem S6 from
(
Philippenko and Dieuleveut 2020
)
and we don’t recall them here.
Hence, we can write that we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V_{k}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma_{k}\mu)\left(\left\|w_{k-1}-w_{*}\right\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{k}^{2}C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|h_{k-1}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right\|^{2}\right)+\frac{2\gamma_{k}^{2}\sigma^{2}\triangle}{N}\,,

and because for any
k
in
N
, the step-size is decreasing, we have
\gamma_{k}\leq\gamma_{k-1}
, which makes to recover the Lyapunov function
V_{k-1}
at step
k-1
:
\mathbb{E}\left[V_{k}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\leq(1-\gamma_{k}\mu)V_{k-1}+\frac{2\gamma_{k}^{2}\sigma^{2}\triangle}{N}
.
Taking full expectation and unrolling the sequence
(V_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}V_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1-\gamma_{i}\mu)V_{0}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}\triangle}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\gamma_{j}^{2}\prod_{i=j+1}^{k}(1-\gamma_{i}\mu)\,.

To show that each part of the bound given in the above equation tends to zero when
k
grows to infinity is classical computations and can be find for instance in lectures notes of
Bach 2022
, and
Kushner and Yin 2003
.

To apply Theorem 1 from
Polyak and Juditsky 1992
, which gives the desired result, it suffices to prove the convergence in probability of the covariance of the noise
\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})
towards
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}
, as
k\to\infty
.

In the following, we show that
\underset{k\to+\infty}{\lim}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{=}}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}
.

Let
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
, for this purpose, we consider the following additive/multiplicative noise decomposition:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*}))\\
&\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k})=H_{F}\eta_{k}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{k-1})-h_{k-1}^{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*}))+h_{k-1}^{i}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(S32)

Furthermore, we have that
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{2}}\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}
because of the Hölder-inequality (
Lemma

1
) and because we shown that
h_{K}^{i}\xrightarrow[K\to+\infty]{L^{2}}\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
; thereby
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{add}}]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{1}}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}
.
Next, from
Equation

S32
, we write:

\displaystyle\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}

\displaystyle=(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1}))(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1}))^{\top}

\displaystyle=\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\otimes\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}-\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{\top}-\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}+\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})\otimes\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})\,.

(i) Developing the covariance of the additive noise and considering
1
and
3
results to
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\otimes\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*}))^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
.
For any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
and any client
i
in
[N]
, we note
\Theta_{i}^{\prime}
the covariance of
g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
, then
g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
is an i.i.d. zero-centered random vectors draw from a distribution
p_{\Theta_{i}^{\prime}}
, hence we have for any iteration
k
in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\otimes\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]=\overline{\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}^{i},(p_{\Theta_{i}^{\prime}})_{i=1}^{N})}\,.

(ii) Second, we show that
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]
converge to 0 in probability: it is sufficient to show that
\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\|_{F}
tends to 0.
To do so, we use the fact that
\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\|_{F}=\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})\|^{2}_{2}
, it results to the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\|H\eta_{k-1}\right\|^{2}+3\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(\textsl{g}_{k}^{i}(w_{k-1})-h_{k-1}^{i})-\mathcal{C}_{k}^{i}(g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*}))\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+3\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{k-1}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})\right\|^{2}\,.

Considering the Hölder inequality given
in
Item

L.2
from
Lemma

1
, because
\eta_{k}\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{2}}0
and
h_{k}^{i}\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{L^{2}}\nabla F_{i}(w_{*})
, we deduce that
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]
tends to
0
in
L^{1}
-norm.

(iii) Third, it remains to show that
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{\mathbb{P}}0
.
We use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality’s
S3
for conditional expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}\|_{F}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})\|_{2}\|(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}\|_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})\|^{2}_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}\|^{2}_{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\,.

The sequence of random vectors
(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is i.i.d., and moreover we have shown previously that
\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}
tends to 0, hence
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{k}^{\mathrm{M}}(\eta_{k-1})(\xi_{k,*}^{\mathrm{A}})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}]
converges to
0
in distribution.
Consequently, noting
\Theta_{i}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}[g_{k,*}^{i}-\nabla F_{i}(w_{*}))^{{\otimes 2}}]
we can state that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi_{k}(\eta_{k-1})^{{\otimes 2}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{\mathbb{P}}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{ania}}^{\infty}=\overline{\mathfrak{C}(\mathcal{C}^{i},(p_{\Theta_{i}^{\prime}})_{i=1}^{N})}\,.
